2-vector bundles
Konrad Waldorf

Abstract
We develop a ready-to-use comprehensive theory for (super) 2-vector bundles over smooth manifolds. It is based on the bicategory of (super) algebras, bimodules, and intertwiners as a model for 2-vector spaces. We discuss symmetric monoidal structures and the corresponding notions of dualizability, and we derive a classification in terms of Cech cohomology with values in a crossed module. One important feature of our 2-vector bundles is that they contain bundle gerbes as well as ordinary algebra bundles as full sub-bicategories, and hence provide a unifying framework for these so far distinct objects. We provide several examples of isomorphisms between bundle gerbes and algebra bundles, coming from representation theory, twisted K-theory, and spin geometry.



\addtotheorempostheadhook
\addtotheorempostheadhook

1
Introduction

In this paper, we develop a theory of 2-vector bundles.
Just as a vector bundle over a manifold
X
is a collection of vector spaces (the fibres), together with information on how these fit together to a bundle structure, a 2-vector bundle will be a geometric object whose fibres are 2-vector spaces.
Here, a 2-vector space is an object of a delooping bicategory of the category of vector spaces, i.e., a symmetric monoidal bicategory such that the endomorphism category of the monoidal unit is equivalent to a vector space category.

In this paper, we choose a certain bicategory of finite-dimensional algebras, finite-dimensional bimodules and intertwiners over
k=\mathbb{R}
or
\mathbb{C}
, which we denote by
2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
. In fact, we work throughout with a larger bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
of
super
2-vector spaces, where algebras and bimodules are
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded and all intertwiners are grading preserving; this is crucial for several applications.
The idea to consider algebras as 2-vector spaces was brought up by Schreiber in discussions at the n-Category Café
[
Sch06
,
Sch07
]
, also see
[
Sch09
, §A]
and
[
SW13
, §4.4]
for early references advocating this point of view.
There are many other possible (mostly “smaller”) choices, see the Appendix of
[
BDSPV
]
for a “bestiary of 2-vector spaces”; e.g., Kapranov-Voevodsky 2-vector spaces
[
KV94
]
. Another round of examples comes from various flavors of linear categories.
In particular, in the context of TQFTs, a frequently used model for 2-vector spaces are linear finitely semisimple abelian categories.
Our reason to use the algebra/bimodule model of 2-vector spaces is that it is, for the most part, straightforward to turn algebras and bimodules into bundles.
Another reason is that many of the examples we have in mind are of this form.

In our previous article
[
KLW
]
we have described how to turn (finite-dimensional) algebras and bimodules into algebra
bundles
and bimodule
bundles
over smooth manifolds, in such a way that a bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
is obtained.
The composition in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
is the relative tensor product of bimodule bundles over an algebra bundle. The well-definedness of such a tensor product does not come for free and depends crucially on the admitted class of bimodules and the conditions one imposes for their local triviality. Our article
[
KLW
]
describes and solves these issues.
In
[
KLW
]
and in the present work, we stick to
finite-dimensional
algebras and bimodules; in our subsequent article
[
KLW22
]
we also consider versions with von Neumann algebras.

The bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
of algebra bundles is a preliminary version of 2-vector bundles. It is preliminary because algebra bundles do not satisfy bicategorical descent – one cannot glue locally defined algebra bundles along invertible bimodule bundles to again obtain an algebra bundle. Another way to say this is that algebra bundles – though locally trivial as bundles – are not locally trivial in the correct bicategorical sense, i.e., as 2-vector bundles.
In more precise terms, we have shown in
[
KLW
]
that the presheaf of bicategories
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
, which assigns to each smooth manifold
X
the bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
, is only a pre-2-stack, but not a 2-stack.
However, every pre-2-stack can be 2-stackified using the plus construction of Nikolaus and Schweigert
[
NS11
]
, and this is precisely our definition (
definition

2.3.2
) of super 2-vector bundles:

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}:=(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k})^{+}.

In particular, our super 2-vector bundles then form a 2-stack, i.e., they do satisfy descent and are locally trivial as 2-vector bundles.

Concretely, a super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}
on a manifold
X
consists of the following data: a surjective submersion
Y\to X
,
a super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
over
Y
; a bundle
\mathcal{M}
of invertible
\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\!\mathcal{A}
-
\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\!\mathcal{A}
-bimodules over
Y^{[2]}
; and an invertible even intertwiner

\mu:\operatorname{pr}_{23}^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{pr}_{12}^{*}\mathcal{M}\to\operatorname{pr}_{13}^{*}\mathcal{M}

over
Y^{[3]}
, which satisfies a coherence condition over
Y^{[4]}
.
Here,
Y^{[k]}
denotes the
k
-fold fibre product of
Y
with itself over
X
, which comes with projection maps
\operatorname{pr}_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}}:Y^{[l]}\to Y^{[k]}
. Schematically, we depict a super 2-vector bundle as

\mathscr{V}=\left(\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern (1.1)

We think of the surjective submersion
Y\to X
in terms of a generalized open cover of
X
:
any open cover
(U_{i})_{i\in I}
of
X
gives rise to a surjective submersion
Y=\coprod_{i\in I}U_{i}\to X
(in fact, a local diffeomorphism); yet, allowing general surjective submersions is often convenient in practice.
We emphasize that every super 2-vector bundle has its individual surjective submersion
Y\to X
, and that in general it cannot be assumed to be trivial (i.e.,
Y=X
).

The isomorphism class of the typical fibre of the algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
in (
1.1
) is not an invariant under isomorphisms of super 2-vector bundles.
However, it turns out that its Morita class is an invariant, which we call the Morita class of the super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}
; it is the higher analog of the rank of an ordinary vector bundle.

For each manifold
X
, we obtain a bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
of super 2-vector bundles on
X
, and one may ask for a classification, i.e., a description of the set of isomorphism classes.
In this article, we classify super 2-vector bundles of fixed Morita class
A
in terms of the first Čech cohomology with values in the automorphism 2-group
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
of
A
.
As a Lie 2-group, the automorphism 2-group
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
can be presented by a smooth crossed module
A^{\times}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\curvearrowleft}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Aut}(A)
, where
A_{0}^{\times}
, the group of even units in
A
, includes into
\mathrm{Aut}(A)
as inner automorphisms, and
\mathrm{Aut}(A)
acts on
A_{0}^{\times}
in the standard way.
Precisely, our classification result is the following; see
theorem

4.2.4
in the main text.

Theorem 1
.

Let
A
be a Picard-surjective super algebra. Then, there is a canonical bijection

\mathrm{h}_{0}\bigl(A\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)\bigr)\cong\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A))\text{,}

i.e., super 2-vector bundles over X of Morita class A are classified by the Čech cohomology of X with values in
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
.

Here,
\mathrm{h}_{0}
denotes the set of isomorphism classes of objects in a bicategory. Moreover, we say that a super algebra
A
is
Picard-surjective
if the natural map
\mathrm{Aut}(A)\to\mathrm{Pic}(A)
is surjective. We proved in
[
KLW
, Prop. A2]
that
every
super algebra is Morita equivalent to a Picard-surjective one; hence, our classification result in fact applies to
all
2-vector bundles.

Though there is a well-defined tensor product between arbitrary 2-vector bundles, the bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
is in general not monoidal.
The problem lies already in the bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
and has been observed and discussed in
[
KLW
]
.
It is rooted in the fact that the well-definedness of the
relative
tensor product between bimodule bundles (the composition) requires a condition (“implementing”) that is not preserved under the
exterior
tensor product of bimodule bundles (the monoidal structure).
In
[
KLW
]
, we suggest two options to solve this problem.
Either, one can take the underlying sub-bigroupoid
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))
; this circumvents above problem because
invertible
bimodule bundles are automatically implementing. Or, one can consider the full sub-bicategory
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
over all
semisimple
super 2-vector bundles; i.e., ones whose super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
has semisimple fibres; here, the problem does not arise as bimodule bundles between
semisimple
super algebras are automatically implementing.

The invertible objects in both symmetric monoidal bicategories (i.e., the objects with tensor inverses) provide a unified picture: we show that in both cases a super 2-vector bundle is invertible if and only if its Morita class is a
central simple super algebra
(
proposition

3.6.2
). Invertible super 2-vector bundles will also be called
super 2-line bundles
; these form a symmetric monoidal bicategory denoted
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
.
Our classification specializes to the following; see
theorem

4.4.4
in the main text.

Corollary 2
.

For any smooth manifold
X
, there is a canonical group isomorphism

\mathrm{h}_{0}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))\cong\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,\mathrm{BW}_{k})\times\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\underline{k}^{\times}).

Here
\mathrm{BW}_{k}
is the Brauer-Wall group for the field
k=\mathbb{R}
or
\mathbb{C}
, and the first factor on the right hand side specifies the Morita class of the super 2-line bundle on each connected component.
This result was previously obtained by Mertsch in his PhD thesis
[
Mer20
]
by a direct computation, whereas we compute the Čech cohomology group
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A))
in case of a central simple super algebra and then deduce the result from
1
.

We also study weaker notions of invertibility in symmetric monoidal bicategories, most importantly
full dualizability
.
While in the bigroupoid
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))
dualizability, full dualizability, and invertibility are the same, we show (
proposition

3.6.2
) that in
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
all objects, i.e., all semisimple super 2-vector bundles are fully dualizable. In this sense, full dualizability corresponds to semisimplicity, just as it is the case for 2-vector
spaces

[
BDSPV
]
.

An important feature of our framework is that both super
algebra bundles
and (super)
bundle gerbes
give rise to super 2-vector bundles. This can be seen directly using the above description in (
1.1
):
for an algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
on
X
, one just takes
Y=X
and
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{A}
to get a 2-vector bundle, while for a bundle gerbe over
k
with surjective submersion
Y\to X
, one just takes
\mathcal{A}=\underline{k}
, the trivial algebra bundle over
Y
with typical fibre
k
. In particular, super 2-vector bundles provide a framework in which isomorphisms between super algebra bundles and bundle gerbes can be discussed.
Thus, questions like “When is a bundle gerbe a bundle of algebras?” (see
[
Pav
]
) obtain a well-defined meaning (and answer: if and only if its Dixmier-Douady class is torsion, see
corollary

4.4.9
).
Throughout this paper, we provide a variety of results about the correlation between super 2-line bundles, super algebra bundles, and super bundle gerbes; see
sections

3.2
,
3.3
and
4.4
.
Figure

1.1
shows schematically a summary of these results.

2-vector bundles
algebra bundles
bundle gerbes
2-line bundles
semisimple
2-vector bundles

Figure 1.1:
A Venn diagram relating various sorts of 2-vector bundles and bundle gerbes. The intersection between algebra bundles and bundle gerbes consists of those algebra bundles whose fibres are Morita equivalent to the ground field, and at the same time of all bundle gerbes whose Dixmier-Douady class is torsion. The intersection between algebra bundles and 2-line bundles consists of all algebra bundles whose fibres are central and simple.

A geometric example of the situation that a bundle gerbe is isomorphic to an algebra bundle is the following: an oriented Riemannian manifold
X^{d}
canonically carries the
spin lifting gerbe

\mathscr{G}_{\operatorname{SO}_{d}}^{\operatorname{Spin}_{d}}
, which is a real line bundle gerbe.
This bundle gerbe is trivial if and only if
X
admits a spin structure, and a trivialization is precisely a choice of spin structure.
On the other hand, one can apply the Clifford algebra construction to the tangent bundle, which yields a bundle
\operatorname{Cl}(TX)
of real super algebras over
X
.
Both the spin lifting gerbe and the Clifford algebra bundle give rise to 2-vector bundles, and they may be compared as such. In fact, we prove that

\mathscr{G}_{\operatorname{SO}_{d}}^{\operatorname{Spin}_{d}}\cong\operatorname{Cl}(TX)\otimes\operatorname{Cl}_{-d}

as 2-vector bundles, where an isomorphism is provided by the twisted spinor bundle.
We derive this from a general statement (
theorem

5.3
) about lifting gerbes and representations, and also prove some variations concerning complex scalars and non-oriented manifolds.

We remark that precursors to our super 2-vector bundles have appeared in several places, but the presentation as the 2-stackification of a presheaf of bicategories (together with the consequential fact that they form a 2-stack) is new in the present paper.
For example, in
[
Pen
]
, Pennig defines “Morita bundle gerbes” with respect to a unital C
∗
-algebra
A
.
If
A
is finite-dimensional, this essentially reduces to our (ungraded) complex 2-vector bundles of Morita class
A
.
Pennig does not define a bicategory (let alone a 2-stack) but only considers stable equivalence classes.
He also classifies Morita bundle gerbes by the Čech cohomology with coefficients in a C
∗
-algebraic version of the 2-group
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
, and our classification theory was strongly motivated by Pennig’s ideas and results.
It is worth pointing out that, in spite of the similarities to Pennig’s approach, our methods are more conceptual as we use the modern 2-stack-theoretical framework.
Moreover, we go a bit further, for instance, with the discussion of the tensor product of algebras under this classification.

In
[
Ers
]
Ershov adapts Pennig’s definition to a finite-dimensional setting. Ershov’s Morita bundle gerbes coincide with our ungraded complex 2-line bundles of Morita class
\mathbb{C}
, see
item

3
for further comparison.
In his lecture notes
[
Fre12
]
Freed sketches the definition of a 2-category of
invertible algebra bundles
over topological groupoids
\mathcal{G}
, which coincides with (a continuous version of) our notion of super 2-line bundles if
\mathcal{G}
is the groupoid obtained from a cover
Y\to X
, i.e., where
\mathcal{G}_{0}=Y
and
\mathcal{G}_{1}=Y^{[2]}
.
Freed then obtains a classification result similar to
2
, using homotopy-theoretical methods. Finally, in
[
BDR04
]
Baas, Dundas, and Rognes propose a definition of a “charted 2-vector bundle” based on a category of 2-vector spaces that is equivalent to Kapranov-Voevodsky 2-vector spaces.
In
[
NS11
]
Nikolaus and Schweigert recast charted 2-vector bundles in term of 2-stackification, showing that charted 2-vector bundles form a 2-stack. Nonetheless, we do not see any direct relation between these charted 2-vector bundles and our 2-vector bundles, and their framework does not seem to capture the geometric examples we have in mind.

2
2-vector bundles

The goal of this section is to introduce the 2-stack of 2-vector bundles in several flavours (e.g., real/complex, ungraded/graded).
We explain first what our models for 2-vector spaces are, and then turn these into bundles over manifolds. This results in a variety of pre-2-stacks which may be viewed as “preliminary 2-vector bundles”.
We then apply the 2-stackification procedure of Nikolaus-Schweigert
[
NS11
]
to construct 2-stacks of 2-vector bundles of various flavours.

2.1
The bicategory of 2-vector spaces

In order to define 2-vector bundles, one first has to settle on a good notion of 2-vector
spaces
. In general,
2
-vector spaces should form a symmetric monoidal bicategory
\mathscr{V}
such that the monoidal category
\mathscr{E}\!\mathrm{nd}_{\mathscr{V}}(\mathbf{1})
of endomorphisms of the monoidal unit
\mathbf{1}
is isomorphic to some category of vector spaces (e.g., finite-dimensional ones) over a field
k
. We describe in this section our choice of a sub-bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
of the symmetric monoidal bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
of finite-dimensional super algebras over
k
, where
k
is either
\mathbb{R}
or
\mathbb{C}
.
We start by recalling the basics of the bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
.

The objects of
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
are monoid objects in the symmetric monoidal category
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
of finite-dimensional super vector spaces; in more detail, these are finite-dimensional,
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded, unital, associative algebras
A
over
k
(in short: super algebras). We will use the notation
A=A_{0}\oplus A_{1}
to denote the graded components.
If
A
and
B
are super algebras, then the 1-morphisms
A\to B
in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
are
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded, finitely generated
B
-
A
-bimodules (we will just say
bimodules
).
The 2-morphisms are bimodule intertwiners, and are always required to be parity-preserving.

If
M:B\to A
and
N:C\to B
are 1-morphisms (i.e.,
M
is a
A
-
B
-bimodule and
N
is a
B
-
C
-bimodule), then the composition in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
is the relative tensor product
M\otimes_{B}N
over
B
, which gives a
A
-
C
-bimodule and hence a 1-morphism
C\to A
.
The reason that 1-morphisms from
B
to
A
are taken to be
A
-
B
-bimodules (as opposed to
B
-
A
-bimodules), is that we then have
M\circ N=M\otimes_{B}N
for the composition of 1-morphisms in our 2-category; consequently we do not have to distinguish between the symbols
\circ
and
\otimes
.
The
identity bimodule
of a super algebra
A
is
A
, considered as an
A
-
A
-bimodule in the obvious way.
An
A
-
B
-bimodule
M
is
invertible
if there exists a
B
-
A
-bimodule
N
such that
M\otimes_{B}N\cong A
and
N\otimes_{A}M\cong B
.
Two super algebras
A
,
B
are isomorphic in the bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
if and only if there exists an invertible
A
-
B
-bimodule
M
; this relation is usually called
Morita equivalence
.

Remark 2.1.1
.

The bicategory
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
of (non-super) algebras, (non-super) bimodules and intertwiners forms a (non-full) sub-bicategory, by declaring algebras and bimodules to be purely even. That way, the ungraded situation is included in our discussion.

We recall that to any
A
-
B
-bimodule
M
one can associate another
A
-
B
-bimodule
\Pi M
with the graded components swapped, see
[
KLW
, §2.1]
.
We also recall the usual way of twisting bimodules by super algebra homomorphisms (i.e., even algebra homomorphisms).
Given a super algebra homomorphism
\phi:A^{\prime}\to A
and an
A
-
B
-bimodule
M
, there is an
A^{\prime}
-
B
bimodule
\prescript{}{\phi}{M}
with underlying vector space
M
, the right
B
-action as before and the left
A^{\prime}
-action induced along
\phi
.
Further, if
\psi:B^{\prime}\rightarrow B
is another super algebra homomorphism, then we obtain a
A
-
B^{\prime}
-bimodule
M_{\psi}
in a similar way.
Both twistings can be performed simultaneously, resulting in an
A^{\prime}
-
B^{\prime}
-bimodule
\prescript{}{\phi}{M}_{\psi}
.
If
N
is another
A^{\prime}
-
B^{\prime}
-bimodule, then an intertwiner
u:N\to\prescript{}{\phi}{M}_{\psi}
is the same as an intertwiner along
\phi
and
\psi
, i.e., an even linear map satisfying

u(a\triangleright n\triangleleft b)=\phi(a)\triangleright u(n)\triangleleft\psi(b),\qquad n\in N,\penalty\ \penalty\ a\in A^{\prime},\penalty\ \penalty\ b\in B^{\prime}.

(2.1.1)

We refer to
[
KLW
, Lem. 2.1.3]
for a summary of further properties of this construction.

For any super algebra
A
, one may consider the Picard group,
\mathrm{Pic}(A)
.
By definition, its elements are the isomorphism classes of invertible
A
-
A
-bimodules, and its group multiplication is given by the relative tensor product over
A
.
If
\phi\in\mathrm{Aut}(A)
, then the bimodule
A_{\phi}
is invertible.
Moreover,
A_{\phi}\otimes_{A}A_{\psi}\cong A_{\phi\circ\psi}
.
Thus, the assignment
\phi\mapsto A_{\phi}
induces a group homomorphism

\mathrm{Aut}(A)\rightarrow\mathrm{Pic}(A).

(2.1.2)

A super algebra is called
Picard-surjective
, if every invertible
A
-
A
-bimodule
M
is isomorphic to one of the form
A_{\phi}
for some
\phi\in\mathrm{Aut}(A)
.
In other words, the map (
2.1.2
) is surjective.

As working with bicategories can be involved due to non-strictness, it is often convenient – when possible – to reduce to a one-categorical context.
This leads to the notion of a
framed bicategory
: a bicategory
\mathscr{B}
together with a category
\mathscr{C}
with the same objects and a functor
\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{B}
such that (a) it is the identity on the level of objects and (b) the image of every morphism of
\mathscr{C}
has a right adjoint in
\mathscr{B}
.
Assigning to a super algebra homomorphism
\varphi:A\to B
the
B
-
A
-bimodule
B_{\varphi}
establishes such a framing

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}_{k}\to\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}

(2.1.3)

for the bicategory of super algebras; see
[
KLW
, Lem. 2.1.6]
for a more detailed discussion.

Another important feature of the bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
is that it is symmetric monoidal in the sense of Schommer-Pries
[
SP09
, Definition 2.3]
. The monoidal structure is the
graded
tensor product of super algebras, respectively, the exterior graded tensor product on bimodules over
k
.
The tensor unit is the field
k
, considered as a trivially graded super algebra,
\mathbf{1}:=k
. This way, we obtain
\mathscr{E}\!\mathrm{nd}_{\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}}(\mathbf{1})\cong\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
as symmetric monoidal categories, which qualifies the bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
as a bicategory of 2-vector spaces.

Remark 2.1.2
.

One can show that every super algebra
A
is dualizable with respect to the symmetric monoidal structure of
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
. Furthermore, a super algebra is
fully
dualizable if and only if it is semisimple (equivalently, separable), see
[
BDSPV
]
. Finally, it is well-known that a super algebra is
invertible
if it is central and simple.
The group of isomorphism classes of invertible objects in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
is called the
Brauer-Wall group
of
k
. It is well known that
\mathrm{BW}_{\mathbb{R}}=\mathbb{Z}_{8}
and
\mathrm{BW}_{\mathbb{C}}=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, and that representatives are the real and complex Clifford algebras,
\operatorname{Cl}_{n}
(
n=0,...,7
) and
\mathbb{C}\!\operatorname{l}_{n}
(
n=0,1
), respectively.

The passage from algebras to algebra bundles is straightforward, whereas the passage from bimodules to bimodule bundles needs extra care, see
section

2.2
.
The crucial point is to achieve a well-defined relative tensor product of bimodule bundles over an algebra bundle.
A locally trivial vector bundle structure on the fibrewise relative tensor product does
not come for free and requires some control of the bimodule actions.
This leads us to the definition of the sub-bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}\subseteq\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
, which will be our choice of 2-vector spaces, and which we explain next.

We associate to any
A
-
B
-bimodule
M
the group
I(M)
of triples
(\phi,u,\psi)
consisting of super algebra automorphisms
\phi\in\mathrm{Aut}(A)
and
\psi\in\mathrm{Aut}(B)
and of an invertible (even) intertwiner
u
along
\phi
and
\psi
, i.e.,
u
satisfies the relation (
2.1.1
).
It is straightforward to show
[
KLW
, §3.1]
that
I(M)
is a Lie subgroup of
\mathrm{Aut}(A)\times\mathrm{GL}(M)\times\mathrm{Aut}(B)
. We call
I(M)
the
group of implementers
, due to the fact that if
(\phi,u,\psi)\in I(M)
, then
u

implements
the automorphisms
\phi
and
\psi
, in the sense that

\phi(a)\triangleright m=u^{-1}(a\triangleright u(m)),\qquad m\triangleleft\psi(b)=u^{-1}(u(m)\triangleleft b),\qquad a\in A,\penalty\ \penalty\ m\in M,\penalty\ \penalty\ b\in B\text{.}

(2.1.4)

We will next consider the projection maps

p_{\ell}:I(M)\to\mathrm{Aut}(A)\quad\text{ and }\quad p_{r}:I(M)\to\mathrm{Aut}(B)\text{,}

(2.1.5)

which are smooth group homomorphisms. The following definition has been introduced in
[
KLW
, Def. 3.1.3]
.

Definition 2.1.3

(Implementing modules)
.

Let
A
and
B
be super algebras and let
M
be an
A
-
B
-bimodule.
Then,
M
is called implementing if the maps
p_{\ell}
and
p_{r}
are open.

We refer to
[
KLW
, §3.1]
for some remarks concerning this definition.
Moreover, we infer from
[
KLW
]
the following results.

Proposition 2.1.4
.

1.

For any automorphisms
\phi,\psi\in\mathrm{Aut}(A)
, the bimodule
\prescript{}{\psi}{A}_{\phi}
is implementing.

2.

Every invertible bimodule is implementing.

3.

Every bimodule over semisimple algebras is implementing.

4.

The relative tensor product of implementing bimodules is implementing.

For examples of non-implementing bimodules, we refer again to
[
KLW
]
.
In particular, if
\phi,\psi:A\to B
are (non-invertible) super algebra homomorphisms, it happens that the bimodule
\prescript{}{\psi}{B}_{\phi}
is
not
implementing.

Due to
item

4
we are in position to define a sub-bicategory

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}\subseteq\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}

with the same objects (super algebras) but only the
implementing
bimodules as 1-morphisms (and all intertwiners between those as 2-morphisms). By
item

2
, both bicategories have the same set of isomorphism classes of objects.
In other words, two algebras are Morita equivalent if and only if they are isomorphic in any of these bicategories.
Moreover, by
item

3
, we still have
\mathscr{E}\!\mathrm{nd}_{\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}}(\mathbf{1})\cong\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
, so that
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
is a valid choice of a bicategory of super 2-vector spaces.
One of the fundamental insights of our paper
[
KLW
]
was that in the context of algebra
bundles
, it is the smaller bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
which succeeds as a model for 2-vector spaces in relation with 2-vector bundles; this is the topic of the next subsection.

Before we proceed, we note that the framing

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}_{k}\to\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}

of
eq.

2.1.3
does not co-restrict to the sub-bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
, because the bimodule
B_{\varphi}
is not necessarily implementing for all algebra homomorphisms
\varphi:A\to B
. One way to restore this is to restrict to the
groupoid

\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}_{k})
of super algebras and super algebra
iso
morphisms. Then, by
item

1
, we obtain a new framing

\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}_{k})\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}\text{.}

(2.1.6)

Another option is to restrict to
semisimple
super algebras, i.e., to the full subcategory
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}_{k}\subseteq\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}_{k}
and to the full sub-bicategory
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
over all semisimple super algebras. Then, by
item

3
we obtain a framing

\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}_{k}\to\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}\text{.}

(2.1.7)

Similarly, we note that the symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
does not restrict to
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
, because the exterior tensor product of implementing bimodules need not be implementing
[
KLW
, Ex. 3.1.5 (4)]
.
However, the symmetric monoidal structure restricts (by
item

2
) to the sub-bigroupoid
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k})
and (by
item

3
) to the sub-bicategory
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
, so that both of these bicategories are framed symmetric monoidal bicategories.

2.2
Algebra bundles and bimodule bundles

We will now set up a bundle version of our bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
of super 2-vector spaces over smooth manifolds, which will be a preliminary, yet incomplete, version of 2-vector bundles.
The larger bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
does not admit such a bundle version as the relative tensor product of perfectly fine but non-implementing bimodule bundles does in general not admit a locally trivial vector bundle structure.

Let
X
be a smooth manifold. The definition of super algebra bundles is standard, and the definition of bimodule bundles is taken from
[
KLW
, §4.1]
.

Definition 2.2.1

(Super algebra bundle)
.

A
super algebra bundle over

X
is a smooth vector bundle
\pi:\mathcal{A}\to X
, with the structure of a super algebra on each fibre
\mathcal{A}_{x}
,
x\in X
, such that each point in
X
has an open neighborhood
U\subseteq X
for which there exists a super algebra
A
and a diffeomorphism
\phi:\mathcal{A}|_{U}\to U\times A
that preserves fibres and restricts to a super algebra isomorphism
\phi_{x}:\mathcal{A}_{x}\to A
in each fibre over
x\in U
.
A
morphism between two super algebra bundles
is a vector bundle morphism that is a grading-preserving algebra homomorphism in each fibre. Super algebra bundles over
X
and homomorphisms form a category
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
.

Remark 2.2.2
.

1.

As defined, super algebra bundles do not necessarily have a single typical fibre, in the sense that the super algebras
A
of all local trivializations could be chosen to be the same. However, a straightforward argument shows that the restriction of a super algebra bundle to a connected component of
X
does have a single typical fibre.

2.

A
trivial super algebra bundle

\mathcal{A}
over
X
is given by a family
\{A_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of super algebras, one for each connected component
X_{i}\subseteq X
, via
\mathcal{A}|_{X_{i}}:=\underline{A}_{i}:=X_{i}\times A_{i}
, the trivial algebra bundle with fibre
A_{i}
.
Note that trivial super algebra bundles canonically pull back to trivial ones.

Definition 2.2.3

(Bimodule bundle)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be super algebra bundles over
X
. An
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule bundle
is a super vector bundle
\mathcal{M}
over
X
with the structure of an
\mathcal{A}_{x}
-
\mathcal{B}_{x}
-bimodule in each fibre
\mathcal{M}_{x}
, such that each point
x\in X
has an open neighborhood
U\subseteq X
over which there exist local trivializations
\phi:\mathcal{A}|_{U}\to U\times A
and
\psi:\mathcal{B}|_{U}\to U\times B
(as super algebra bundles), an
A
-
B
-bimodule
M
, and a local trivialization
u:\mathcal{M}|_{U}\to U\times M
(as a vector bundle) that is fibrewise an intertwiner along
\phi
and
\psi
.
If
X
can be covered by open sets supporting local trivializations with the same
A
,
B
, and
M
, then we say that
\mathcal{M}
has
typical fibre
the triple
(A,M,B)
.
Morphisms
between
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule bundles are super vector bundle morphisms that are even intertwiners in each fibre (they will again be called intertwiners).

\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule bundles and their morphisms form a category
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{im}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}(X)
.

Example 2.2.4
.

1.

Let
\phi:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
be an isomorphism of super algebra bundles over
X
. Then, there is a
\mathcal{B}
-
\mathcal{A}
-bimodule bundle
\mathcal{B}_{\phi}
, which over a point
x\in X
has fibres
(\mathcal{B}_{x})_{\phi_{x}}
.
Its typical fibre is
(B,B_{\varphi},A)
, where
A
and
B
are typical fibres of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
, respectively, and
\varphi:A\to B
is an arbitrary super algebra isomorphism, see
[
KLW
, Ex. 4.1.5]
.
We remark that this does
not
generalize to non-invertible super algebra bundle homomorphisms
\phi:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
, see
[
KLW
, Ex. 4.3.3 & 4.3.4]
.

2.

If
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
are trivial super algebra bundles over
X
, defined by families
\{A_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and
\{B_{i}\}_{i\in I}
as in
remark

2.2.2
, then a
trivial
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule bundle
is given by a family
\{M_{i},\phi_{i},\psi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
with
A_{i}
-
B_{i}
-bimodules
M_{i}
and smooth maps
\varphi_{i}:X_{i}\to\mathrm{Aut}(A_{i})
and
\psi_{i}:X_{i}\to\mathrm{Aut}(B_{i})
, one for each connected component
X_{i}\subseteq X
, via
\mathcal{M}|_{X_{i}}:={}_{\psi_{i}}{\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.84473pt}}}\end{picture}M_{i}\hskip-0.42677pt}_{\,\phi_{i}}
.
Every bimodule bundle is locally isomorphic to a trivial one.

For a deeper discussion of bimodule bundles we refer to
[
KLW
, §4.1]
. It turns out that there is no relative tensor product of bimodule bundles as defined above (see
section

2.2
,
[
KLW
, §4.2]
).
We proved in
[
KLW
, Thm. 4.2.6]
that the following additional constraint fixes this problem.

Definition 2.2.5

(Implementing bimodule bundle)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be super algebra bundles over
X
. An
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule bundle
\mathcal{M}
is called
implementing
if all
fibres
\mathcal{M}_{x}
are implementing. Implementing
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule bundles and their morphisms form a full subcategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{im}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}(X)
of
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{im}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}(X)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
, and
\mathcal{C}
be super algebra bundles, let
\mathcal{M}
be an implementing
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule bundle and
\mathcal{N}
an implementing
\mathcal{B}
-
\mathcal{C}
-bimodule bundle. In
[
KLW
, Prop. 4.2.3]
we have constructed the relative tensor product
\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{N}
, which is an implementing
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{C}
-bimodule bundle. Thus, we have a functor

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{im}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(X)\times\mathrm{s}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{im}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(X)\to\mathrm{s}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{im}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(X)\text{.}

(2.2.1)

It is unproblematic to construct associators and unitors for this tensor product functor, turning it into the composition of 1-morphisms in a bicategory whose objects are super algebra bundles.

Definition 2.2.6

(Bicategory of super algebra bundles)
.

The
bicategory of super algebra bundles
over
X
, denoted
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
, has objects super algebra bundles over
X
, 1-morphisms implementing bimodule bundles over
X,
and 2-morphisms even intertwiners.
The composition is given by the relative tensor product (
2.2.1
).

Super algebra bundles are a preliminary version of super 2-vector bundles: they (only) form a
pre-
2-stack
[
KLW
, Prop. 4.5.1]
, while
the true super 2-vector bundles that we define in
section

2.3
form a 2-stack (
theorem

2.3.3
).

We recall from
remark

2.1.2
that the bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
has two interesting sub-bicategories, the sub-bicategory
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k})
and the full sub-bicategory
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
over all semisimple super algebras. They are symmetric monoidal and come with (symmetric monoidal) framings

\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}_{k})\to\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k})\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}_{k}\to\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}

discussed in
eqs.

2.1.6
and
2.1.7
. We recall that
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
itself is not symmetric monoidal, and also not framed by
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}_{k}
.

This picture passes without changes from super 2-vector
spaces
to the bicategory of super algebra
bundles
.
In the first case, we define the sub-bicategory

\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X))\subseteq\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)

containing only the invertible bimodule bundles (note that by
[
KLW
, Lem. 4.2.8]
, “invertible” is equivalent to “fibrewise invertible”) and the invertible intertwiners.
Then,
example

2.2.4
furnishes a functor

\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))\to\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X))

(2.2.2)

which is indeed a framing and symmetric monoidal
[
KLW
, Lem. 4.3.1]
. For semisimple algebras, we first define the full sub-category
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)\subseteq\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
over all super algebra bundles with semisimple fibres, and similarly, the full sub-bicategory
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)\subseteq\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
. If
\varphi:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is a homomorphism of semisimple super algebra bundles, then by
[
KLW
, Prop. 4.3.5]
, we obtain a well-defined
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule bundle
\mathcal{B}_{\varphi}
, and hence, a functor

\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)\to\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)\text{.}

(2.2.3)

By
[
KLW
, Cor. 4.3.6]
, this is again a framing and symmetric monoidal.
The following result is
[
KLW
, Cor. 4.4.4]
.

Proposition 2.2.7
.

The following table describes the dualizable, fully dualizable, and invertible objects in both symmetric monoidal categories of preliminary super 2-vector bundles:

dualizable

fully dualizable

invertible

\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X))

central simple

central simple

central simple

\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)

all

all

central simple

Remark 2.2.8
.

Both symmetric monoidal bicategories
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X))
and
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
have a second symmetric monoidal structure given by the direct sum of algebra bundles, and the exterior direct sum of bimodule bundles. The two monoidal structures are compatible with each other in the sense of distributive laws, and form a
commutative rig bicategory
.

We denote by

\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)\subseteq\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)

the full sub-bicategory over all super algebra bundles with central simple fibres. It will turn out to be a preliminary version of super 2-
line
bundles.
Note that
\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
is symmetric monoidal with the induced tensor product.
A result of Donovan-Karoubi
[
DK70
, Theorems 6 and 11]
shows the following.

Theorem 2.2.9
.

There is a canonical bijection

\mathrm{h}_{0}(\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X))\cong\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,\mathrm{BW}_{k})\times{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\mathrm{Tor}(\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\underline{k}^{*}))\text{,}

where
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}
denotes Čech cohomology,
\underline{k}^{*}
is the sheaf of smooth
k^{*}
-valued functions and the last group is the torsion subgroup of
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\underline{k}^{*})
. Moreover, this bijection becomes an isomorphism of groups upon defining the group structure on the right hand side by

(\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\cdot(\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\beta_{2}):=(\alpha_{0}+\beta_{0},\alpha_{1}+\beta_{1},(-1)^{\alpha_{1}\cup\beta_{1}}\alpha_{2}\beta_{2})\text{.}

(2.2.4)

Remark 2.2.10
.

1.

The cup product is viewed here as a map

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\to{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\to\mathrm{Tor}(\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\underline{k}^{*}))

with the second arrow induced by the inclusion
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\to k^{*}:x\mapsto(-1)^{x}
.

2.

We have
\mathrm{Tor}(\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\underline{\mathbb{C}}^{*}))=\mathrm{Tor}(\mathrm{H}^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}))
and
\mathrm{Tor}(\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\underline{\mathbb{R}}^{*}))=\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{2})
.

Example 2.2.11
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be a Riemannian vector bundle over
X
with typical fibre a Euclidean vector space
V
, let
\operatorname{Cl}(\mathcal{V})
be the associated bundle of Clifford algebras, and let
\mathbb{C}\!\operatorname{l}(\mathcal{V}):=\operatorname{Cl}(\mathcal{V})\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}
be its complexification. Both bundles,
\operatorname{Cl}(\mathcal{V})
and
\mathbb{C}\!\operatorname{l}(\mathcal{V})
, are invertible by
proposition

2.2.7
, and their classes under the classification of
theorem

2.2.9
are
([\operatorname{Cl}(V)],w_{1}(\mathcal{V}),w_{2}(\mathcal{V}))
and
([\mathbb{C}\!\operatorname{l}(V)],w_{1}(\mathcal{V}),W_{3}(\mathcal{V}))
respectively, where
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
are the Stiefel-Whitney classes and
W_{3}
is the third integral Stiefel-Whitney class
[
DK70
, Lemma 7]
.

Remark 2.2.12
.

Ungraded algebra bundles are again treated as a special case of super algebra bundles concentrated in even degrees.
They form a bicategory
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
.
The subcategories
\mathrm{ss}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
and
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X))
are symmetric monoidal and framed by
\mathrm{ss}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
and
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))
, respectively.
The invertible objects form a full sub-bicategory
\mathrm{cs}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
. The ungraded version of
theorem

2.2.9
was also proved by Donovan-Karoubi
[
DK70
, Theorems 3 and 8]
, and gives a group isomorphism

\mathrm{h}_{0}(\mathrm{cs}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X))\cong\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,\mathrm{Br}_{k})\times\mathrm{Tor}(\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\underline{k}^{*}))\text{,}

with the direct product group structure on the right hand side.
Here,
\mathrm{Br}_{k}
denotes the Brauer group of the field
k
.

2.3
The 2-stack of 2-vector bundles

One of the most important features any notion of bundles should have, is that bundles can be glued together from locally defined pieces.
In other words, bundles satisfy
descent
, or form a
stack
. In a bicategorical setting, there is a corresponding notion of a
2-stack
.
Super algebra bundles and bimodule bundles as in
definition

2.2.6
form a
pre-2-stack
, but in general not a 2-stack. In this section, we will explain how to solve this issue.
For a general proper discussion of (pre-)2-stacks we refer to
[
NS11
]
.

First of all, super algebra bundles and bimodule bundles can be pulled back along smooth maps in a coherent fashion. This can be phrased by saying that the assignment

X\mapsto\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)

forms a
presheaf of bicategories
over the category of smooth manifolds. We will denote this presheaf by
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
.
It has two important sub-presheaves:

1.

The sub-presheaf
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
where only the semisimple super algebra bundles are admitted. This is a presheaf of symmetric monoidal framed bicategories.

2.

The further sub-presheaf
\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
, where only the central simple super algebra bundles are admitted.

Further, we have corresponding versions of ungraded presheaves of bicategories.

Remark 2.3.1
.

To make the notion of bicategorical descent a bit more explicit, let us look for the moment at the presheaf
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{\mathbb{C}}
of ungraded, complex algebra bundles, bimodule bundles, and intertwiners.
Consider an open cover
\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha}
of a smooth manifold
X
.
Suppose a family
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}
of algebra bundles over
U_{\alpha}
is given, together with invertible
\mathcal{A}_{\beta}
-
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}
-bimodule bundles
\mathcal{M}_{\alpha\beta}
over
U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}
, and invertible intertwiners
\mu_{\alpha\beta\gamma}:\mathcal{M}_{\beta\gamma}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{\beta}}\mathcal{M}_{\alpha\beta}\to\mathcal{M}_{\alpha\gamma}
over
U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\cap U_{\gamma}
that satisfy the obvious associativity condition over 4-fold intersections.
For the presheaf
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{\mathbb{C}}
to be a 2-stack, there must exist a globally defined algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
together with invertible
\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}
-
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}
-bimodule bundles
\mathcal{N}_{\alpha}
and invertible intertwiners

\varphi_{\alpha\beta}:\mathcal{N}_{\beta}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{\beta}}\mathcal{M}_{\alpha\beta}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{\alpha}

that are compatible with the isomorphisms
\mu_{\alpha\beta\gamma}
in an obvious way.
However, this is not necessarily the case (see
remark

4.4.7
).
Our current knowledge is the following:

•

The presheaves
\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{\mathbb{R}}
and
\mathrm{cs}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{\mathbb{R}}
are 2-stacks; this will be proved in
corollary

4.4.6
.

•

The presheaves
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{\mathbb{C}}
,
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{\mathbb{C}}
,
\mathrm{cs}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{\mathbb{C}}
, and
\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{\mathbb{C}}
are not 2-stacks; this will be proved in
remark

4.4.7
.

•

Currently, we do not know whether or not the presheaves
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{\mathbb{R}}
and
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{\mathbb{R}}
are 2-stacks.

To impose order on this chaos, we choose to 2-stackify
all
these pre-2-stacks, even those that are 2-stacks already.
In those cases where we already have a 2-stack, 2-stackification gives an equivalent 2-stack with more objects, and it turns out that these additional objects are often useful.

For the 2-stackification of presheaves
\mathscr{F}
of bicategories we use the plus construction
\mathscr{F}\mapsto\mathscr{F}^{+}
of Nikolaus-Schweigert
[
NS11
]
.

Definition 2.3.2

(2-vector bundle)
.

We define the following presheaves of bicategories of super 2-vector bundles:

•

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}:=(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k})^{+}
is the presheaf of
super 2-vector bundles
over
k
.

•

\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}:=(\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k})^{+}
is the presheaf of
semisimple super 2-vector bundles
over
k
.

•

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}:=(\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k})^{+}
is the presheaf of
super 2-line bundles
over
k
.

Additionally, we define the following presheaves of bicategories of (ungraded) 2-vector bundles:

•

2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}:=(\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k})^{+}
is the presheaf of
2-vector bundles
over
k
.

•

\mathrm{ss}\text{-}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}:=(\mathrm{ss}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k})^{+}
is the presheaf of
semisimple 2-vector bundles
over
k
.

•

2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}:=(\mathrm{cs}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k})^{+}
is the presheaf of
2-line bundles
over
k
.

Below we spell out explicitly all details of the plus construction. Before that, we shall state its main purpose.

Theorem 2.3.3
.

All presheaves of bicategories defined in
definition

2.3.2
are 2-stacks.

This follows from
[
NS11
, Thm. 3.3]
, whose only condition is that the presheaves are pre-2-stacks, which we proved in
[
KLW
, Prop. 4.5.1]
.
∎

For the convenience of the reader (and the authors) we shall now spell out all definitions in case of the bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
of super 2-vector bundles over
X
, on the basis of the description of the plus construction given in
[
NS11
]
. The other versions of 2-vector bundles can then easily be obtained as sub-bicategories:

•

\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
is the sub-bicategory where all super algebra bundles are bundles of semisimple super algebras.

•

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
is the sub-bicategory where all super algebra bundles are bundles of central simple super algebras.

•

The corresponding ungraded versions consist of only ungraded algebras and ungraded bimodules.

This way, our explanations below apply to all four cases by employing the corresponding restrictions.

I.) Objects.

A
super 2-vector bundle
over
X
is a quadruple
\mathscr{V}=(\pi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
consisting of:

•

a surjective submersion
\pi:Y\to X
,

•

a super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
over
Y
,

•

an invertible bimodule bundle
\mathcal{M}
over
Y^{[2]}
whose fibre
\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{2}}
over a point
(y_{1},y_{2})\in Y^{[2]}
is an
\mathcal{A}_{y_{2}}
-
\mathcal{A}_{y_{1}}
-bimodule, and

•

an invertible even intertwiner
\mu
of bimodule bundles over
Y^{[3]}
, which restricts over each point
(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})\in Y^{[3]}
to an
\mathcal{A}_{y_{3}}
-
\mathcal{A}_{y_{1}}
-intertwiner

\mu_{y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}}:\mathcal{M}_{y_{2},y_{3}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{2}}\to\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{3}}\text{.}

This structure is subject to the condition that

\mu
is
associative
, i.e., the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{y_{3},y_{4}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{y_{3}}}\mathcal{M}_{y_{2},y_{3}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\otimes\mu_{y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{y_{2},y_{3},y_{4}}\otimes 1}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{y_{2},y_{4}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{y_{3},y_{4}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{y_{3}}}\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{y_{1},y_{3},y_{4}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{4}}}

is commutative for all
(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})\in Y^{[4]}
.

We remark the following additional facts, which are easy to deduce: first, if
\Delta:Y\to Y^{[2]}
denotes the diagonal map, then there exists a canonical invertible intertwiner
\Delta^{*}\mathcal{M}\cong\mathcal{A}
of
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{A}
-bimodule bundles over
Y
. Second, if
s:Y^{[2]}\to Y^{[2]}
swaps the factors, then
s^{*}\mathcal{M}
is inverse to
\mathcal{M}
.

The simplicial structure of a super 2-vector bundle may be depicted as the following diagram:

II.) 1-morphisms.

Let
\mathscr{V}_{1}=(\pi_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{1},\mu_{1})
and
\mathscr{V}_{2}=(\pi_{2},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{M}_{2},\mu_{2})
be super 2-vector bundles over
X
. A
1-morphism

\mathscr{P}:\mathscr{V}_{1}\to\mathscr{V}_{2}

is a triple
\mathscr{P}=(\zeta,\mathcal{P},\phi)
consisting of:

•

a surjective submersion
\zeta:Z\to Y_{1}\times_{X}Y_{2}
,

•

an implementing bimodule bundle
\mathcal{P}
over
Z
, whose fibre
\mathcal{P}_{z}
over a point
z\in Z
with
\zeta(z)=:(y_{1},y_{2})
is an
(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}
-
(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}
-bimodule, and

•

an invertible even intertwiner
\phi
of bimodule bundles over
Z^{[2]}
, which restricts over a point
(z,z^{\prime})\in Z^{[2]}
with
\zeta(z)=:(y_{1},y_{2})
and
\zeta(z^{\prime})=:(y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime})
to an
(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}^{\prime}}
-
(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}
-intertwiner

\phi_{z,z^{\prime}}:\mathcal{P}_{z^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime}}\to(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{P}_{z}\text{.}

This structure is subject to the condition that the intertwiner
\phi
is a “homomorphism” with respect to the intertwiners
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
, i.e., the diagram

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 85.35062pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-85.35062pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{z^{\prime\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}^{\prime\prime}}}(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.00786pt\raise 7.28055pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.96945pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes(\mu_{1})_{y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}^{\prime\prime}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 165.35074pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0389pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime}}\otimes\operatorname{id}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 213.6068pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0389pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{z,z^{\prime\prime}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 213.6068pt\raise-56.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}^{\prime}}}\mathcal{P}_{z^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-36.21469pt\raise-48.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0389pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes\phi_{z,z^{\prime}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-56.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 210.6068pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-82.39227pt\raise-64.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{P}_{z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 93.75613pt\raise-71.28055pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
