167.80562pt\raise-64.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 167.80562pt\raise-64.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{P}_{z}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

(2.3.1)

is commutative for all
(z,z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})\in Z^{[3]}
, where
\zeta(z^{\prime\prime})=:(y_{1}^{\prime\prime},y_{2}^{\prime\prime})
.

III.) Identity 1-morphisms.

The
identity 1-morphism
of a super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}=(\pi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
is the triple
\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{V}}:=(\operatorname{id}_{Y^{[2]}},\mathcal{M},\phi_{\mu})
, where
Z:=Y^{[2]}
and

(\phi_{\mu})_{y_{1},y_{2},y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime}}:=\mu^{-1}_{y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime}}\circ\mu_{y_{1},y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime}}\text{.}

IV.) Composition of 1-morphisms.

Consider three super 2-vector bundles and two 1-morphisms

\textstyle{\mathscr{V}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{P}_{12}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{V}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{P}_{23}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{V}_{3}\text{,}}

for whose structure we use the same letters as in above definitions. The
composition

\mathscr{P}_{23}\circ\mathscr{P}_{12}:=(\zeta,\mathscr{P},\phi)
is defined as follows. We set
Z:=Z_{12}\times_{Y_{2}}Z_{23}
, and consider a point
z:=(z_{12},z_{23})\in Z
with
\zeta_{12}(z_{12})=:(y_{1},y_{2})
and
\zeta_{23}(z_{23})=:(y_{2},y_{3})
. The surjective submersion
\zeta:Z\to Y_{1}\times_{X}Y_{3}
is then
\zeta(z):=(y_{1},y_{3})
. The bimodule bundle
\mathscr{P}
is defined so that its fibre over the point
z\in Z
is the
(\mathcal{A}_{3})_{y_{3}}
-
(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}
-bimodule

\mathcal{P}_{z}:=(\mathcal{P}_{23})_{z_{23}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}(\mathcal{P}_{12})_{z_{12}}\text{.}

Finally, the intertwiner
\phi
is over a point
((z_{12},z_{23}),(z_{12}^{\prime},z_{23}^{\prime}))\in Z^{[2]}
defined by

\textstyle{\hskip-100.00015pt\mathcal{P}_{z_{12}^{\prime},z_{23}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}}\mathcal{M}_{1}=(\mathcal{P}_{23})_{z^{\prime}_{23}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{P}_{12})_{z_{12}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}}\mathcal{M}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\circ(\phi_{12})_{z_{12},z_{12}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{P}_{23})_{z_{23}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}^{\prime}}}\mathcal{M}_{2}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}(\mathcal{P}_{12})_{z_{12}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\phi_{23})_{z_{23},z_{23}^{\prime}}\circ\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{3}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{3})_{y_{3}}}(\mathcal{P}_{23})_{z_{23}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}(\mathcal{P}_{12})_{z_{12}}=\mathcal{M}_{3}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{3})_{y_{3}}}\mathcal{P}_{z_{12},z_{23}}\text{.}\hskip-100.00015pt}

V.) 2-morphisms.

Consider two 1-morphisms between the same super 2-vector bundles,

\textstyle{\mathscr{V}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{P}}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{P}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{V}_{2}\text{.}}

For abbreviation, we set
Y_{12}:=Y_{1}\times_{X}Y_{2}
.
Then, a
2-morphism

\mathscr{P}\Rightarrow\mathscr{P}^{\prime}
is represented by pairs
(\rho,\varphi)
consisting of the following structure:

•

a surjective submersion
\rho:W\to Z\times_{Y_{12}}Z^{\prime}
, and

•

an intertwiner
\varphi
of bimodule bundles over
W
that restricts over a point
w\in W
with
\rho(w)=:(z,z^{\prime})
to an intertwiner

\varphi_{w}:\mathcal{P}_{z}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{z^{\prime}}

of
(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}
-
(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}
-bimodules, where
\zeta(z)=\zeta^{\prime}(z^{\prime})=:(y_{1},y_{2})
.

This structure is subject to the condition that
\varphi
commutes with the intertwiners
\phi
and
\phi^{\prime}
, i.e., the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{z}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{\tilde{y}_{1}}}(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1},\tilde{y}_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\tilde{w}}\otimes\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{z,\tilde{z}}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2},\tilde{y}_{2}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{P}_{z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes\varphi_{w}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\tilde{z}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{\tilde{y}_{1}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{y}_{1}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\prime}_{z^{\prime},\tilde{z}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2}^{\prime},\tilde{y}_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y^{\prime}_{2}}}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{z^{\prime}}}

is commutative for all
(w,\tilde{w})\in W\times_{X}W
, where
\rho(w)=(z,z^{\prime})
,
\rho(\tilde{w})=(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime})
.
Two pairs
(\rho,\varphi)
and
(\rho^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
have to be identified if the pullbacks of
\varphi
and
\varphi^{\prime}
coincide over
W\times_{Z\times_{Y_{12}}Z^{\prime}}W^{\prime}
.
Since in that sense the choice of
\rho
is unimportant, we usually denote the 2-morphism by just
\varphi
.

VI.) Vertical composition of 2-morphisms.

Next, consider three 1-morphisms between the same super 2-vector bundles, and two 2-morphisms:

\textstyle{\mathscr{V}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{P}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{P}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{P}^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{V}_{2}\text{.}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\prime}}

We suppose that the 1-morphisms
\mathscr{P}
,
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}
, and
\mathscr{P}^{\prime\prime}
come with surjective submersions
\zeta:Z\to Y_{12}
,
\zeta^{\prime}:Z^{\prime}\to Y_{12}
, and
\zeta^{\prime\prime}:Z^{\prime\prime}\to Y_{12}
, respectively, and that the 2-morphisms
\varphi
and
\varphi^{\prime}
come with surjective submersions
\rho:W\to Z\times_{Y_{12}}Z^{\prime}
and
\rho^{\prime}:W^{\prime}\to Z^{\prime}\times_{Y_{12}}Z^{\prime\prime}
, respectively.
We consider
W\times_{Z^{\prime}}W^{\prime}
equipped with the surjective submersion
(w,w^{\prime})\mapsto(z,z^{\prime\prime})
, where
(z,z^{\prime}):=\rho(w)
and
(z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime}):=\rho^{\prime}(w^{\prime})
. Then the vertical composition
\varphi^{\prime}\bullet\varphi:\mathscr{P}\Rightarrow\mathscr{P}^{\prime\prime}
is the intertwiner over
W\times_{Z^{\prime}}W^{\prime}
defined fibrewise over
(w,w^{\prime})
by

\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{w}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{z^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\prime}_{w^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}_{z^{\prime\prime}}}
.

VII.) Identity 2-morphisms.

The identity 2-morphism
\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{P}}
of a 1-morphism
\mathscr{P}=(\zeta,\mathcal{P},\phi)
is obtained by restricting the intertwiner
\phi
to
W:=Z\times_{Y_{12}}Z\subseteq Z\times_{X}Z
. Over
(z_{1},z_{2})
with
\zeta(z_{1})=\zeta(z_{2})=(y_{1},y_{2})
, this becomes an intertwiner,

\mathcal{P}_{z_{1}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}}(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1},y_{1}}\to(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2},y_{2}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{P}_{z_{2}}\text{.}

Under the canonical invertible intertwiners
(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}\cong(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1},y_{1}}
and
(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}\cong(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{2},y_{2}}
, this yields an intertwiner
\varphi
with
\varphi_{z_{1},z_{2}}:\mathcal{P}_{z_{1}}\to\mathcal{P}_{z_{2}}
, and the pair
(\operatorname{id}_{W},\varphi)
is the identity 2-morphism of
\mathscr{P}
.

VIII.) Horizontal composition of 2-morphisms.

Consider the following super 2-vector bundles, 1-morphisms, and 2-morphisms,

\textstyle{\mathscr{V}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{P}_{12}}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{P}_{12}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{V}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{P}_{23}}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{23}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{V}_{3}\text{,}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{12}}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{23}}

with all structure labelled as above. The horizontal composition of
\varphi_{12}
and
\varphi_{23}
, denoted
\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12}
, is defined by the smooth manifold
W:=W_{12}\times_{Y_{2}}W_{23}
equipped with the surjective submersion
(w_{12},w_{23})\mapsto((z_{12},z_{23}),(z_{12}^{\prime},z_{23}^{\prime}))
, where
(z_{12},z_{12}^{\prime}):=\rho_{12}(w_{12})
and
(z_{23},z_{23}^{\prime}):=\rho_{23}(w_{23})
, and the intertwiner of bimodule bundles over
W
, given in the fibre over
(w_{12},w_{23})\in W
by

\textstyle{(\varphi_{23})_{w_{23}}\otimes(\varphi_{12})_{w_{12}}:(\mathcal{P}_{23})_{z_{23}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}(\mathcal{P}_{12})_{z_{12}}\to(\mathcal{P}_{23}^{\prime})_{z_{23}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}(\mathcal{P}_{12}^{\prime})_{z_{12}^{\prime}}\text{.}}

This completes the explicit description of super 2-vector bundles.
We close this section with four useful technical results about super 2-vector bundles, which follow directly from the plus construction
[
NS11
]
, and are well known, e.g., for bundle gerbes.

Lemma 2.3.4
.

1.

A 1-morphism
\mathscr{P}=(\zeta,\mathcal{P},\phi)
is invertible if and only if its bimodule bundle
\mathcal{P}
is invertible.

2.

A 1-morphism
\mathscr{P}=(\zeta,\mathcal{P},\phi)
has a right (left) adjoint if and only if its bimodule bundle
\mathcal{P}
has a right (left) adjoint.

3.

A 2-morphism
\varphi
is invertible if and only if its intertwiner
\varphi
is invertible.

Remark 2.3.5
.

Bimodule bundles are invertible (have adjoints) if and only if they are fibrewise invertible (have fibrewise adjoints)
[
KLW
, Lem. 4.2.8]
.
Thus, the conditions in
lemma

2.3.4
can be checked fibrewise.

Remark 2.3.6
.

lemma

2.3.4
also has the following consequence. We may consider as in
section

2.2
the
sub-presheaf
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k})
where only invertible bimodule bundles and invertible intertwiners are admitted. Applying the plus construction, we see by
item

3
that all resulting 2-morphisms are invertible, and we see by
item

1
that all resulting 1-morphisms are invertible. Thus, we have

\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k})^{+}=\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k})\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k})^{+}=\mathrm{Grpd}(2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k})\text{.}

In other words, it does not matter if we truncate to 2-groupoids before or after 2-stackification.

Our second result shows that the surjective submersions of 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms can be assumed to be identities.

Lemma 2.3.7
.

1.

Every 1-morphism is 2-isomorphic to one with
Z=Y_{1}\times_{X}Y_{2}
and
\zeta=\operatorname{id}_{Z}
.

2.

Every 2-morphism can be represented by a pair
(\rho,\varphi)
with
W=Z\times_{Y_{12}}Z^{\prime}
and
\rho=\operatorname{id}_{W}
.

Lemma

2.3.7
makes use of the fact that
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
is a pre-2-stack. The reason that our definitions above allow for general
Z
and
W
is that all kinds of compositions result in such more general choices, and in practice it is often easier to keep those instead of performing descent.
Our third result allows to refine the surjective submersion of a super 2-vector bundles without changing its isomorphism class.

Lemma 2.3.8
.

If
\mathscr{V}=(\pi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
is a super 2-vector bundle with surjective submersion
\pi:Y\to X
, and
\rho:Y^{\prime}\to Y
is a smooth map such that
\pi^{\prime}:=\pi\circ\rho
is again a surjective submersion, then
\mathscr{V}^{\rho}:=(\pi^{\prime},\rho^{*}\mathcal{A},(\rho^{[2]})^{*}\mathcal{M},(\rho^{[3]})^{*}\mu)
is another super 2-vector bundle, and there exists a canonical isomorphism
\mathscr{V}^{\rho}\cong\mathscr{V}
.

The canonical isomorphism
\mathscr{V}^{\rho}\to\mathscr{V}
is best viewed in terms of the framing of the bicategory of super 2-vector bundles that we introduce below in
section

3.5
. A consequence is the following result, referring to the triviality of super algebra bundles and bimodule bundles defined in
remarks

2.2.2
and
2.2.4
.

Proposition 2.3.9
.

Every super 2-vector bundle is isomorphic to one with trivial super algebra bundle and trivial bimodule bundle.

We first show that the super algebra bundle can assumed to be trivial.
Let
\mathscr{V}=(\pi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
be a super 2-vector bundle. Let
\{U_{\alpha}\}
be an open cover of
X
that admits local sections
\sigma_{\alpha}:U_{\alpha}\to Y
such that
\sigma_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{*}}\mathcal{A}\cong U_{\alpha}\times A_{\alpha}
.
Let
Y^{\prime}
be the disjoint union of the open sets
U_{\alpha}
, let
\pi^{\prime}:Y^{\prime}\to X
be the canonical projection, and let
\sigma:Y^{\prime}\to Y
be given by
\sigma|_{U_{\alpha}}:=\sigma_{\alpha}
.
Then, we have
\pi^{\prime}=\pi\circ\sigma
, and by
lemma

2.3.8
,
\mathscr{V}
is isomorphic to a super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}^{\sigma}
with trivial algebra bundle.

Now let
\mathscr{V}=(\pi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
be a super 2-vector bundle with trivial super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
.
Let
\{U_{\alpha}\}
be an open cover of
Y^{[2]}
with connected sets
U_{\alpha}
over which the bimodule bundle
\mathcal{M}
trivializes, i.e.,
\mathcal{M}|_{U_{\alpha}}\cong{}_{\varphi_{\alpha}}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 14.2142pt}}}\end{picture}M_{\alpha}\hskip-0.42677pt{}_{\,\psi_{\alpha}}
, where
M_{\alpha}
is an
A_{i}
-
A_{j}
-bimodule,
\varphi_{\alpha}:U_{\alpha}\to\mathrm{Aut}(A_{i})
, and
\psi_{\alpha}:U_{\alpha}\to\mathrm{Aut}(A_{j})
, for
i
labelling the connecting component of
Y
containing
\operatorname{pr}_{1}(U_{\alpha})
, and
j
labelling the one containing
\operatorname{pr}_{2}(U_{\alpha})
.
On paracompact spaces such as manifolds, such a hypercover of height 1 can be refined by an ordinary cover; i.e., there exists an open cover
\{W_{i}\}
of
X
with smooth sections
\sigma_{i}:W_{i}\to Y
, such that for each non-trivial double intersection
W_{i}\cap W_{j}
there exists an index
\alpha
with
\sigma_{i}(W_{i})\times_{X}\sigma_{j}(W_{j})\subseteq U_{\alpha}
.
Proceeding as above, and using
lemma

2.3.8
,
\mathscr{V}
is isomorphic to a super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}^{\sigma}
with trivial super algebra bundle and trivial bimodule bundle.
∎

3
Properties of 2-vector bundles

In this section we discuss a number of structures and features of 2-vector bundles. We will mostly stick to the 2-stack
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}
and only consider its sub-2-stacks
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}
and
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}
when necessary or interesting.

3.1
The Morita class of a 2-vector bundle

Definition 3.1.1

(Morita class)
.

Let
\mathscr{V}=(\pi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
be a super 2-vector bundle over
X
, and let
A
be a super algebra. We say that
\mathscr{V}

is of Morita class
A
if around every point
y\in Y
there exists a local trivialization
\mathcal{A}|_{U}\cong U\times A_{U}
with a super algebra
A_{U}
that is Morita equivalent to
A
.

In
[
KLW
, Def. 4.2.9]
we have also introduced the notion of a Morita class for super algebra bundles; in that sense, a super 2-vector bundle is of Morita class
A
if and only if its super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
is of Morita class
A
.
The following result describes the behaviour of the Morita class of a super 2-vector bundle. It exhibits the Morita class as a generalization of the rank of an ordinary vector bundle.

Lemma 3.1.2
.

Let
\mathscr{V}
be a super 2-vector bundle.

1.

If
X
is connected, then there exists a super algebra
A
such that
\mathscr{V}
is of Morita class
A
.
In fact,
\mathscr{V}
is of Morita class
\mathcal{A}_{y}
, where
\mathcal{A}_{y}
is the fibre of
\mathcal{A}
over any point
y\in Y
.

2.

Let
A
and
B
be super algebras, and suppose
\mathscr{V}
is of Morita class
A
.
Then,
\mathscr{V}
is of Morita class
B
if and only if
A
and
B
are Morita equivalent.

3.

Let
\mathscr{V}_{1}
,
\mathscr{V}_{2}
be two super 2-vector bundles with
\mathscr{V}_{1}\cong\mathscr{V}_{1}
. Then, for any super algebra
A
,
\mathscr{V}_{1}
is of Morita class
A
if and only if
\mathscr{V}_{2}
is of Morita class
A
.

(a) For each connected component
Y_{i}
of
Y
, there exists by
[
KLW
, Lem. 4.2.10 (a)]
a super algebra
A_{i}
such that
\mathcal{A}|_{Y_{i}}
is of Morita class
A_{i}
.
If
Y_{i}
and
Y_{j}
are components such that
\pi(Y_{i})\cap\pi(Y_{j})\neq\emptyset
, then there exists a point
(y_{i},y_{j})\in Y^{[2]}
with
y_{i}\in Y_{i}
and
y_{j}\in Y_{j}
.
Then
\mathcal{M}_{y_{i},y_{j}}
is a Morita equivalence between
\mathcal{A}_{y_{2}}
and
\mathcal{A}_{y_{1}}
, showing that
A_{i}
and
A_{j}
are Morita equivalent.
If
Y_{i}
and
Y_{j}
are arbitrary connected components, then, since
X
is connected, there exists a finite sequence
Y_{i}=Y_{a_{1}},...,Y_{a_{n}}=Y_{j}
of connected components of
Y
such that
\pi(Y_{a_{k}})
and
\pi(Y_{a_{k+1}})
intersect.
This shows that
\mathcal{A}
is of Morita class
A_{i}
, for any connected component
Y_{j}
of
Y
.

(b) is trivial.
For (c), suppose that
\mathscr{V}_{1}
and
\mathscr{V}_{2}
are presented in terms of surjective submersions
Y_{1}
, respectively
Y_{2}
.
Let
\mathscr{P}=(\zeta,\mathcal{P},\phi)
be an isomorphism between
\mathscr{V}_{1}
and
\mathscr{V}_{2}
.
Then for
z\in Z
,
\mathcal{P}_{z}
is a
(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}
-
(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}
-bimodule, where
\zeta(z)=(y_{1},y_{2})
.
As
\mathscr{P}
is an isomorphism,
\mathcal{P}_{z}
is an invertible bimodule, by
item

1
, i.e., a Morita equivalence.
Hence if
\mathscr{V}_{1}
is of Morita class
A
meaning that
(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}
is Morita equivalent to
A
, then so is
\mathscr{V}_{2}
.
∎

In case of super 2-
line
bundles over
k=\mathbb{R},\mathbb{C}
, the classification of central simple super algebras implies that the Morita class of a
complex
super 2-line bundle can be either
\mathbb{C}\!\operatorname{l}_{0}
or
\mathbb{C}\!\operatorname{l}_{1}
, and the Morita class of a
real
super 2-line bundle can be one of
\operatorname{Cl}_{0},...,\operatorname{Cl}_{7}
.
The following lemma shows that the converse is also true.

Lemma 3.1.3
.

Let
\mathscr{V}
be a super 2-vector bundle of Morita class
\mathbb{C}\!\operatorname{l}_{n}
(for
n=0,1
and
k=\mathbb{C}
) or of Morita class
\operatorname{Cl}_{n}
(for
n=0,...,7
and
k=\mathbb{R}
). Then,
\mathscr{V}
is a super 2-line bundle.

We discuss the complex case, the real case is analogous. By definition of Morita class of super 2-vector bundles, the super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
of
\mathscr{V}
is of Morita class
\mathbb{C}\!\operatorname{l}_{n}
. Thus, each fibre of
\mathcal{A}
is a central simple super algebra, and thus, by
[
KLW
, Prop. 4.4.3]
,
\mathcal{A}
is invertible. This proves that
\mathscr{V}
is an object in
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
. ∎

Remark 3.1.4
.

The Morita class of an
ungraded
2-vector bundle is of course an ungraded algebra (i.e., a super algebra concentrated in degree zero).
Conversely, however, if the Morita class of a super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}
happens to be an ungraded algebra, it is
not
necessarily true that
\mathscr{V}
lies in the sub-bicategory
2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
, as it may still have a non-trivially graded bimodule bundle
\mathcal{M}
over
Y^{[2]}
.
One example where this happens are super line bundle gerbes, considered as super 2-line bundles, see
section

3.2
.

Sometimes it will be convenient to consider only super 2-vector bundles of a fixed Morita class.

Definition 3.1.5

(2-vector bundles with fixed Morita class)
.

Let
A
be a super algebra over
k
. Then, the presheaf
A\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}
of
super 2-vector bundles of Morita class
A
is defined to be the full sub-presheaf of
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}
over all super 2-vector bundles of Morita class
A
.

Remark 3.1.6
.

1.

The presheaf
A\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}
is again a 2-stack, since descent preserves the typical fibres of algebra bundles. In fact, we may consider the presheaf of bicategories
A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}
with all super algebra bundles whose fibres are Morita equivalent to
A
; then,
A\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}=(A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}})^{+}
.

2.

If
A
is an ungraded algebra, then a 2-stack
A\text{-}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}
is obtained in the same way. We remark that, for
A
ungraded, there is a functor
A\text{-}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}\to A\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}
that is in general not essentially surjective, as ungraded algebras may have invertible bimodules that are not concentrated in even degrees.
Graded bundle gerbes provide examples, as follows from the classification result of
lemma

4.4.1
.

3.

For a smooth manifold
X
, the bicategory
\mathbb{C}\text{-}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)
is equivalent to the bicategory of Morita bundle gerbes of Ershov
[
Ers
]
. Concerning the objects, Ershov only allows ungraded matrix algebra bundles
\mathcal{A}
over
Y
, which are precisely the algebra bundles of Morita class
\mathbb{C}
. One difference is that Ershov allows only surjective submersions coming from open covers; this, however, is unproblematic in view of
lemma

2.3.8
. In fact, Ershov considers all Morita bundle gerbes, 1-morphisms, and 2-morphisms with respect to the
same
fixed open cover, which gives an equivalent bicategory to ours when this cover is
good
.

3.2
Inclusion of bundle gerbes

Let
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
be the symmetric monoidal category of super vector bundles over
X
. We denote by
\mathscr{B}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
the corresponding bicategory with a single object.
Then,
X\mapsto\mathscr{B}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
is a pre-2-stack; this is just a reformulation of the fact that vector bundles form a monoidal stack. Its stackification is, by definition
[
NS11
]
, the 2-stack of
super line bundle gerbes
,

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{k}:=(\mathscr{B}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k})^{+}\text{.}

Super line bundle gerbes can be identified with the twistings of complex K-theory defined by Freed-Hopkins-Teleman
[
FHT11
]
; the following statement has been proved by Mertsch
[
Mer20
]
.

Proposition 3.2.1
.

The homotopy 1-category
\mathrm{h}_{1}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathbb{C}}(X))
of the bicategory of complex super line bundle gerbes is canonically equivalent to the category of twistings of complex K-theory defined by Freed-Hopkins-Teleman.

Put differently, the presheaf of categories defined by Freed-Hopkins-Teleman extends to a presheaf of bicategories, and that presheaf is in fact a 2-stack.

Next, we describe the relation between super line bundle gerbes and super 2-vector bundles. We consider the obvious inclusion

\mathscr{B}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}\to\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}

(3.2.1)

of presheaves of bicategories, taking the single object over a manifold
X
to the trivially graded super algebra bundle
\underline{k}
over
X
, and considering super vector bundles as
\underline{k}
-
\underline{k}
-bimodule bundles (they are implementing due to
item

3
). This is fully faithful over each smooth manifold
X
.
By functoriality of the plus construction, we obtain the following result.

Proposition 3.2.2
.

The inclusion in
eq.

3.2.1
induces under the plus construction a fully faithful morphism

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{k}\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}

of 2-stacks. In other words, super line bundle gerbes form a full sub-bicategory of super 2-line bundles.

Explicitly, the 2-stack morphism of
proposition

3.2.2
simply adds to the structure of a given super line bundle gerbe the trivial algebra bundle
\underline{k}
over the domain
Y
of its surjective submersion.

Example 3.2.3
.

The
trivial bundle gerbe

\mathscr{I}
(consisting of the trivial surjective submersion
\operatorname{id}_{X}
, the trivial super line bundle
\mathcal{M}:=\underline{k}
over
X^{[2]}=X
, and the line bundle isomorphism
\mu:\mathcal{M}\otimes\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}
induced by multiplication in
k
) corresponds under the 2-functor
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{k}(X)\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
to the
trivial 2-vector bundle
(consisting of the trivial surjective submersion
\operatorname{id}_{X}
, the trivial super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}:=\underline{k}
, and the same
\mathcal{M}
and
\mu
as before). Since there is no need to distinguish between the trivial bundle gerbe and the trivial 2-vector bundle, we will henceforth denote both by
\mathscr{I}
.

Proposition 3.2.4
.

Let
\mathscr{V}
be a super-2-vector bundle. Then,
\mathscr{V}
is isomorphic to a super line bundle gerbe if and only if
\mathscr{V}
is of Morita class
k
.

The “only if”-part is clear.
Suppose
\mathscr{V}
is of Morita class
k
.
Then by
proposition

2.3.9
,
\mathscr{V}
is isomorphic to a super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}^{\prime}
whose super algebra bundle is the trivial bundle
\underline{k}
over
Y
.
Its bimodule bundle is then a bundle of invertible
k
-
k
-bimodules, i.e., a super line bundle.
This shows that
\mathscr{V}^{\prime}
is a super line bundle gerbe.
∎

Proposition

3.2.4
shows that the fully faithful morphism of
proposition

3.2.2
is not an isomorphism of 2-stacks, since the Morita class of a general super 2-line bundle may be any central simple super algebra. The relation between super line bundle gerbes and super 2-line bundles is further clarified in
section

4.4
.

In the contexts of twisted K-theory and 2-dimensional sigma models, one considers 1-morphisms
\mathscr{E}:\mathscr{G}\to\mathscr{I}
between a super line bundle gerbe
\mathscr{G}
and the trivial bundle gerbe
\mathscr{I}

[
BCM+02
,
GR02
]
. These are often called
bundle gerbe modules
or
\mathscr{G}
-twisted (super) vector bundles
, see
[
Wal07b
]
. An immediate consequence of
proposition

3.2.2
is a reformulation in terms of morphisms between super 2-line bundles.

Corollary 3.2.5
.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a super line bundle gerbes over
X
. Then, the category
\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}_{\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{k}(X)}(\mathscr{G},\mathcal{I})
of
\mathscr{G}
-twisted super vector bundles is canonically isomorphic to the category
\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}_{\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)}(\mathscr{G},\mathcal{I})
of super 2-line bundle morphisms from
\mathscr{G}
to
\mathscr{I}
.

For later use, let us spell out explicitly what a
\mathscr{G}
-twisted super vector bundle is, consulting above definition of a 1-morphism (and using
item

1
). Suppose
\mathscr{G}=(\pi,\mathcal{M},\mu)
. Then, a
\mathscr{G}
-twisted super vector bundle
\mathscr{E}
is a pair
(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon)
consisting of a super vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
over
Y
and a super vector bundle isomorphism
\varepsilon:\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{M}\to\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{E}
over
Y^{[2]}
such that

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern (3.2.2)

commutes.
Likewise, a morphism of
\mathscr{G}
-twisted super vector bundles
(\mathcal{E}_{1},\varepsilon_{1})
and
(\mathcal{E}_{2},\varepsilon_{2})
is a super vector bundle morphism
\varphi:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
over
Y
such that

\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{y_{2}}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{y_{1},y_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{y_{1}}\otimes\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{y_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{y_{1}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{y_{2}}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon^{\prime}_{y_{1},y_{2}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{y_{1}}}

commutes.

Remark 3.2.6
.

If
\mathscr{L}
is a general super 2-line bundle, then a 1-morphism
\mathscr{E}:\mathscr{L}\to\mathscr{I}
generalizes in a natural way the notion of a twisted vector bundle, now admitting more general twistings. In twisted K-theory, these more general twistings add to the ordinary twistings considered by Freed-Hopkins-Teleman the
grading twist
. This is also explained in the lecture notes
[
Fre12
]
and in
[
Mer20
]
.

Remark 3.2.7
.

In the ungraded case, the analogous definition

\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{k}:=(\mathscr{B}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k})^{+}

results in the usual definition of line bundle gerbes precisely as originally defined by Murray (for
k=\mathbb{C}
)
[
Mur96
]
. It induces a fully faithful morphism

\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{k}\to 2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}

(3.2.3)

of 2-stacks,
so that every line bundle gerbe is an example of a 2-vector bundle.
The analog of
proposition

3.2.4
holds: an ungraded 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}
is a bundle gerbe if and only if it is of Morita class
k
.

3.3
Inclusion of algebra bundles

Since the plus construction is 2-stackification, it comes equipped with a fully faithful functor
[
NS11
, Thm. 3.3]
from the pre-2-stack to the 2-stack. In our case, we obtain a fully faithful functor

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)

(3.3.1)

including our “preliminary” super 2-vector bundles into the
true
super 2-vector bundles. We recall that the objects of
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
are super algebra bundles over
X
. Hence, super algebra bundles are examples of 2-vector bundles.

In detail, a super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
over
X
is sent to the super 2-vector bundle
(\operatorname{id}_{X},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A},\mu)
, where we identify the fibre products
X^{[k]}
over
X
with
X
, and
\mu
is the canonical invertible intertwiner
\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{A}\cong\mathcal{A}
induced by the multiplication in
\mathcal{A}
.
We denote this 2-vector bundle again by
\mathcal{A}
.
We also recall that the 1-morphisms
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
are implementing
\mathcal{B}
-
\mathcal{A}
-bimodule bundles
\mathcal{M}
over
X
. Such a bimodule bundle is sent to the 1-morphism of 2-vector bundles given by
(\operatorname{id}_{X},\mathcal{M},\phi)
, where
Z:=X\times_{X}X
and
Z^{[k]}
are again identified with
X
, and
\phi
is the canonical invertible intertwiner

\mathcal{B}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{M}\cong\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{A}
. Finally, the 2-morphisms in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
directly yield 2-morphisms in
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
.

The statement that the functor (
3.3.1
) is fully faithful means that it induces an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{im}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(X)\cong\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}_{\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)}({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{B}})\text{.}

In particular, two super algebra bundles are Morita equivalent if and only if they are isomorphic as 2-vector bundles.

Example 3.3.1
.

The trivial super algebra bundle
\underline{k}
over
X
coincides under the inclusion of
eq.

3.3.1
with the trivial 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{I}
of
example

3.2.3
.

Remark 3.3.2
.

If a super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
has a typical fibre
A
, then the corresponding super 2-vector bundle is of Morita class
A
. This shows that the Morita class cannot distinguish between general super 2-vector bundles and those coming from super algebra bundles. Using the classification we develop in
section

4
we will obtain a result that allows one to determine for a super 2-vector bundle whose Morita class is a central simple super algebra, whether or not it comes from a super algebra bundle, see
corollary

4.4.8
.

Now that we are able to consider bundle gerbes
and
super algebra bundles as 2-vector bundles, we may discuss their relation. A nice structure that relates bundle gerbes and algebra bundles is the following extension of the notion of a twisted super vector bundle (see
section

3.2
).

Definition 3.3.3

(Twisted module bundle)
.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a super line bundle gerbe and let
\mathcal{A}
be a super algebra bundle over
X
.
A
\mathscr{G}
-twisted
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
is
a
\mathscr{G}
-twisted super vector bundle
\mathscr{E}=(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon)
together with a left
\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}
-module bundle structure on
\mathcal{E}
, such that
\varepsilon
is
\mathcal{A}
-linear. A morphism between
\mathscr{G}
-twisted
\mathcal{A}
-module bundles is an
\mathcal{A}
-linear morphism of
\mathscr{G}
-twisted super vector bundles.

This structure appears in an infinite-dimensional setting in
[
KW20
, Def. 2.3.9]
. The following result generalizes
corollary

3.2.5
and shows that the notion of a twisted module bundle can now be absorbed in the bicategory of super 2-vector bundles. Let
\mathscr{E}=(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon)
be a
\mathscr{G}
-twisted
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle. In order to obtain from
\mathscr{E}
a 1-morphism
\mathscr{G}\to{\mathcal{A}}
we identify
Y
with the common refinement
Y\times_{X}X
of the coverings of
\mathscr{G}
and
{\mathcal{A}}
, set
Z:=Y
and
\zeta=\operatorname{id}_{Z}
. Since
\mathcal{E}_{y}
is a left
\mathcal{A}_{x}
-module, where
x=\pi(y)
, it is a
\mathcal{A}_{x}
-
k
-bimodule. Since
\varepsilon
is linear and
\pi^{*}\mathcal{A}
-linear, we may consider it as an
\mathcal{A}_{x}
-
k
-intertwiner

\epsilon_{y_{1},y_{2}}:\mathcal{E}_{y_{2}}\otimes_{k}\mathcal{L}_{y_{1},y_{2}}\to\mathcal{A}_{x}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{x}}\mathcal{E}_{y_{1}}\text{.}

Thus,
\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{E}}:=(\zeta,\mathcal{E},\varepsilon)
is a 1-morphism
\mathscr{G}\to{\mathcal{A}}
.

Lemma 3.3.4
.

The assignment
\mathscr{E}\mapsto\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{E}}
establishes an isomorphism between the category of
\mathscr{G}
-twisted
\mathcal{A}
-module bundles and the category
\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}_{\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)}(\mathscr{G},{\mathcal{A}})
of super 2-vector bundle morphisms from
\mathscr{G}
to
{\mathcal{A}}
. Moreover,
\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{E}}:\mathscr{G}\to{\mathcal{A}}
is invertible if and only if the fibres
\mathcal{E}_{y}
are Morita equivalences between
k
and
\mathcal{A}_{\pi(y)}
, for all
y\in Y
.

It is straightforward to extend above construction to a functor, and to show that it is an equivalence. The invertibility statement follows from
lemma

2.3.4
and
[
KLW
, Lem. 4.2.8 (c)]
.
∎

Remark 3.3.5
.

\mathscr{G}
-twisted
\mathcal{A}
-module bundles can be untwisted by a trivialization of
\mathscr{G}
.
Indeed, if
\mathscr{T}
is such trivialization, i.e., a 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{T}:\mathscr{G}\to\mathscr{I}
, and
\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{E}}:\mathscr{G}\to{\mathcal{A}}
is the 1-morphism that corresponds to a
\mathscr{G}
-twisted
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
\mathscr{E}
under the isomorphism of
lemma

3.3.4
, then
\mathscr{E}^{\mathscr{T}}:=\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{E}}\circ\mathscr{T}^{-1}:\mathscr{I}\to{\mathcal{A}}
is a 1-morphism between super 2-vector bundles in the image of the inclusion of algebra bundles.
Since this inclusion functor is fully faithful,
\mathscr{E}^{\mathscr{T}}
corresponds canonically to a 1-morphism
\underline{k}\to\mathcal{A}
in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
, i.e., to a right
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle.
Summarizing, this procedure turns a
\mathscr{G}
-twisted
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
\mathscr{E}
into an
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
\mathscr{E}^{\mathscr{T}}
over
X
, using the trivialization
\mathscr{T}
.

Remark 3.3.6
.

Given a
\mathscr{G}
-twisted
\mathcal{A}
-module bundle
\mathscr{E}
, it is possible to forget the
\mathcal{A}
-module structure and just keep a
\mathscr{G}
-twisted vector bundle.
Under the identification of
\mathscr{E}
with a 1-morphism
\mathscr{E}:\mathscr{G}\to{\mathcal{A}}
, this corresponds to the composition with the canonical, but non-invertible 1-morphism
{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathscr{I}
obtained as the image of the
\underline{k}
-
\mathcal{A}
-bimodule bundle
\mathcal{A}
under the inclusion of
eq.

3.3.1
.

Given a general super line bundle gerbe
\mathscr{G}
, one may try to construct an algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
with a 1-morphism
\mathscr{G}\to{\mathcal{A}}
.
One method, which applies to lifting gerbes and uses representation theory, is described in
section

5
.
Another method is the following.
Suppose a non-zero
\mathscr{G}
-twisted super vector bundle
\mathscr{E}=(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon)
is given.
Then, the endomorphism bundle
\underline{\mathrm{End}}(\mathcal{E})=\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}^{*}
descends using
\varepsilon
to a super algebra bundle over
X
, which we denote by
\mathrm{End}(\mathscr{E})
.
By construction,
\mathscr{E}
becomes then a
\mathscr{G}
-twisted
\mathrm{End}(\mathscr{E})
-module bundle, and certainly,
\mathcal{E}
is fibrewise a Morita equivalence. Hence, we have the following consequence of
lemma

3.3.4
.

Corollary 3.3.7
.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a super line bundle gerbe over
X
, and suppose
\mathscr{E}
is a non-zero
\mathscr{G}
-twisted super vector bundle. Then,
\mathscr{E}
induces a 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{G}\cong\mathrm{End}(\mathscr{E})
in
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
.

A nice argument
[
BCM+02
, Prop. 4.1]
shows that a (super) bundle gerbe
\mathscr{G}
admits a non-zero
\mathscr{G}
-twisted vector bundle if and only if its Dixmier-Douady class in
\mathrm{H}^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is torsion.
Thus, any torsion bundle gerbe is isomorphic to an algebra bundle.
The converse is also true. Both statements will be proved in a different way later, see
corollary

4.4.9
.

3.4
The fibres of a 2-vector bundle

In the introduction we claimed that a (super) 2-vector bundle is a structure whose fibres are (super) 2-vector spaces.
Suppose
\mathscr{V}
is a super 2-vector bundle over
X
, and
x\in X
. Then, the fibre of
\mathscr{V}
at
x
is defined to be the pullback of
\mathscr{V}
along the map
x:\ast\to X
. We shall thus analyze what a super 2-vector bundle over the point is.

A super 2-vector bundle over a point
X=\{\ast\}
is a tuple
(Y,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
consisting of a smooth manifold
Y
, a super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
over
Y
, an invertible
\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\!\mathcal{A}
-
\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\!\mathcal{A}
-bimodule bundle
\mathcal{M}
over
Y^{2}=Y\times Y
, and an invertible intertwiner
\mu:\operatorname{pr}_{23}^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{pr}_{12}^{*}\mathcal{M}\to\operatorname{pr}_{13}^{*}\mathcal{M}
over
Y^{3}
that is associative over
Y^{4}
. Similarly, we obtain notions of 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms of super 2-vector bundles over a point, and we may consider the bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(\ast)
.

We consider the functor
eq.

3.3.1
that includes super algebra bundles into super 2-vector bundles over a point
X=\{\ast\}
, obtaining a functor

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(\ast)\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(\ast)\text{.}

(3.4.1)

We note that the bicategory of super algebra bundles over a point coincides on the nose with the bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
of super 2-vector spaces.
Moreover, we have the following result.

Lemma 3.4.1
.

The functor of
eq.

3.4.1
establishes an equivalence of categories,

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}\cong\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(\ast)\text{.}

We know already from
section

3.3
that the functor is fully faithful; hence it remains to show that it is essentially surjective. Indeed, suppose
\mathscr{V}=(Y,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
is an object in
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(\ast)
. Choose a point
y_{0}\in Y
, and let
A:=\mathcal{A}_{y_{0}}
. We show that
A
is an essential preimage for
\mathscr{V}
. We consider
Z:=Y\cong\ast\times_{\ast}Y
, equipped with the surjective submersion
\zeta:=\operatorname{id}_{Y}
. Over
Z
we consider the invertible bimodule bundle
\mathcal{P}:=\Delta_{1}^{*}\mathcal{M}
, where
\Delta_{l}:Y\to Y^{l}
is defined by
\Delta_{l}(y_{1},...,y_{l})=(y_{0},y_{1},...,y_{l})
.
Finally, we consider over
Z^{[2]}=Y^{2}
the intertwiner
\phi:=\Delta_{2}^{*}\mu
, which we may view fibrewise as an intertwiner

\phi_{y,y^{\prime}}:\mathcal{P}_{y^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}A\to\mathcal{M}_{y,y^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{y}}\mathcal{P}_{y}\text{.}

It is straightforward to see that
(\zeta,\mathcal{P},\phi)
is a 1-morphism
A\to\mathscr{V}
, and it follows from
item

1
that it is a 1-
iso
morphism.
∎

In view of the equivalence of
lemma

3.4.1
, the fibres of a super 2-vector bundle are super 2-vector spaces, as we claimed in the introduction. We remark that, by
item

1
, all fibres of a super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}
(over a connected base manifold) are isomorphic as super 2-vector spaces, and that they are all isomorphic to the Morita class of
\mathscr{V}
. In this sense, the Morita class may also be viewed as the typical fibre of
\mathscr{V}
.

3.5
Framing by refinements

As discussed in
section

2.2
(see
eqs.

2.2.2
and
2.2.3
), the bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
of super algebra bundles is framed under the groupoid
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))
of super algebra bundles over
X
and bundle
iso
morphisms,
while the bicategory
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}(X)
of
semisimple
super algebra bundles is framed under the category
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
of semisimple super algebra bundles and
all
bundle homomorphisms.
These framings are obviously compatible with pullbacks, and hence morphisms of pre-2-stacks

\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k})\to\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}\to\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}\text{.}

(3.5.1)

We will use the obvious terminology to say that a
framing for a presheaf of bicategories

\mathscr{F}
is a presheaf of categories
\mathscr{E}
together with a morphism
\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{F}
of presheaves of bicategories such that over every smooth manifold
X
, the functor
\mathscr{E}(X)\to\mathscr{F}(X)
is framing. In this situation, we will also call
\mathscr{F}
a
framed presheaf of bicategories
. In this sense, we see that
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
and
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
are
framed pre-2-stacks
.
Observe that the second pre-2-stack is smaller but has a larger framing on the level of morphisms.

Framings are important because they provide a convenient and simple way to construct 1-morphisms, in situations where the structure of a general 1-morphism is fairly complex, as in the case of super 2-vector bundles.
The plus construction automatically sends framed pre-2-stacks to framed 2-stacks. More explicitly, if
\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{F}
is a framed pre-2-stack, then there exists a general procedure to associate to
\mathscr{E}
another presheaf of categories
\mathscr{E}^{\mathscr{F}}
together with a morphism
\mathscr{E}^{\mathscr{F}}\to\mathscr{F}^{+}
turning
\mathscr{F}^{+}
into a framed 2-stack.
To avoid confusion, we remark that this procedure,
\mathscr{E}\mapsto\mathscr{E}^{\mathscr{F}}
, is
not
the plus construction or another method of stackification. In fact,
in many cases,
\mathscr{E}
is already a stack – as in
eq.

3.5.1
. Instead, the construction of
\mathscr{E}^{\mathscr{F}}
depends on the framing
\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{F}
; after all,
\mathscr{E}^{\mathscr{F}}
needs to have the same objects as
\mathscr{F}^{+}
in order to be eligible for a framing. We will describe the general definition of
\mathscr{E}^{\mathscr{F}}
elsewhere; below we will only spell it out in the present cases of the framed pre-2-stacks of
eq.

3.5.1
.

We start with the following basic construction. Given a smooth manifold
X
, we define a category
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}(X)
as follows.
The objects are all super 2-vector bundles
\mathscr{V}
over
X
.
The morphisms will be called
refinements
, defined as follows.

Definition 3.5.1

(Refinement)
.

Let
\mathscr{V}_{1}=(\pi_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{1},\mu_{1})
and
\mathscr{V}_{2}=(\pi_{2},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{M}_{2},\mu_{2})
be super 2-vector bundles over
X
.
A
refinement

\mathscr{R}:\mathscr{V}_{1}\to\mathscr{V}_{2}
is a
triple
\mathscr{R}=(\rho,\phi,u)
consisting of a smooth map
\rho:Y_{1}\to Y_{2}
such that
\pi_{2}\circ\rho=\pi_{1}
, of a homomorphism
\phi:\mathcal{A}_{1}\to\rho^{*}\mathcal{A}_{2}
of super algebra bundles over
Y_{1}
and
of an invertible bundle morphism
u:\mathcal{M}_{1}\to\rho^{*}\mathcal{M}_{2}
over
Y_{1}^{[2]}

that over a point
(y,y^{\prime})\in Y_{1}^{[2]}
restricts to an intertwiner

u_{y,y^{\prime}}:(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y,y^{\prime}}\to(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y),\rho(y^{\prime})}

along the algebra homomorphisms
\phi_{y^{\prime}}:(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y^{\prime}}\to(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{\rho(y^{\prime})}
and
\phi_{y}:(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y}\to(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{\rho(y)}
, and renders the diagram

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 81.97456pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-56.65305pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
