0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.77501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u_{y^{\prime},y^{\prime\prime}}\otimes u_{y,y^{\prime}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 91.74188pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\circ\mu_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 152.37367pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 152.37367pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y,y^{\prime\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 174.23256pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.11528pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u_{y,y^{\prime\prime}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 174.23256pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-81.97456pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y^{\prime}),\rho(y^{\prime\prime})}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{\rho(y^{\prime})}}(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y),\rho(y^{\prime})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 99.20313pt\raise-38.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mu_{2}\circ\operatorname{id}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 141.97466pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 141.97466pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y),\rho(y^{\prime\prime})}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

(3.5.2)

commutative for all
(y,y^{\prime},y^{\prime\prime})\in Y_{1}^{[3]}
.

Given two refinements
\mathscr{R}_{12}=(\rho_{12},\phi_{12},u_{12}):\mathscr{V}_{1}\to\mathscr{V}_{2}
and
\mathscr{R}_{23}=(\rho_{23},\phi_{23},u_{23}):\mathscr{V}_{2}\to\mathscr{V}_{3}
, their composition is given by

\mathscr{R}_{23}\circ\mathscr{R}_{12}:=(\rho_{23}\circ\rho_{12},\rho_{12}^{*}\phi_{23}\circ\phi_{12},(\rho_{12}^{[2]})^{*}u_{23}\circ u_{12})\text{,}

and the identity morphism of
\mathscr{V}
is
(\operatorname{id}_{Y},\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{A}},\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{M}})
.
This defines the category
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}(X)
. It is clear that everything is compatible with pullbacks, and so
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}
is a presheaf of categories.

For each manifold
X
, let
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{inv}\text{-}\mathrm{ref}}_{k}(X)
be the subcategory of
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}(X)
with all super 2-vector bundles and only those refinements whose algebra bundle homomorphism
\phi
is invertible. Further, we let
\mathrm{ss}\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}(X)
be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}(X)
over all semisimple super 2-vector bundles. They assemble to sub-presheaves
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{inv}\text{-}\mathrm{ref}}_{k}
and
\mathrm{ss}\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}
of
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}
, and these are the presheaves
\mathscr{E}^{\mathscr{F}}
in the above general notation, explicitly, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{inv}\text{-}\mathrm{ref}}_{k}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k})^{\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{ss}\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}^{\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}}

The functors
\mathscr{E}^{\mathscr{F}}\to\mathscr{F}^{+}
, explicitly,

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{inv}\text{-}\mathrm{ref}}_{k}\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{ss}\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}\to\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k},

(3.5.3)

are defined as follows.
Working over a manifold
X
, they are, of course, the identity on the level of objects.

On the level of morphisms, they associate to a refinement
\mathscr{R}=(\rho,\phi,u):\mathscr{V}_{1}\to\mathscr{V}_{2}
the following 1-morphism
(\zeta,\mathcal{P},\phi^{\prime})
. We define
Z:=Y_{1}\times_{X}Y_{2}
and
\zeta=\operatorname{id}_{Z}
. Consider the smooth map
\tilde{\rho}:Z\to Y_{2}^{[2]}
with
\tilde{\rho}(y_{1},y_{2}):=(\rho(y_{1}),y_{2})
.
We define
\mathcal{P}:=(\tilde{\rho}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\phi}
over
Z
.
If
\phi
is invertible, this is an implementing bimodule bundle by
example

2.2.4
, while if
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
have semisimple fibres, it is an implementing bimodule bundle by
item

3
.
Over a point
(y_{1},y_{2})\in Z
, its fibre is
\mathcal{P}_{y_{1},y_{2}}=((\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}),y_{2}})_{\phi_{y_{1}}}
, which is indeed an
(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}
-
(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}
-bimodule.
Finally, we define the intertwiner
\phi^{\prime}
fibrewise over a point
((y_{1},y_{2}),(y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime}))\in Z^{[2]}
by

\textstyle{\hskip-130.0002pt\mathcal{P}_{y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime}}=((\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}^{\prime}),y_{2}^{\prime}})_{\phi_{y_{1}^{\prime}}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes u_{y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}^{\prime}),y_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}^{\prime})}}((\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}),\rho(y_{1}^{\prime})})_{\phi_{y_{1}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mu_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}),\rho(y_{1}^{\prime}),y_{2}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{((\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}),y_{2}^{\prime}})_{\phi_{y_{1}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mu_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}),y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime}}^{-1}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}((\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y_{1},)y_{2}})_{\phi_{y_{1}}}=(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{P}_{y_{1},y_{2}}\text{.}\hskip-130.0002pt}

It is tedious though absolutely straightforward to check that
(\zeta,\mathcal{P},\phi^{\prime})
defined like this is a 1-morphism
\mathscr{V}_{1}\to\mathscr{V}_{2}
.
Even more, it turns out that this assignment indeed defines a functor, and moreover, using
item

2
, that every 1-morphism obtained from a refinement has a right adjoint.
In total, we obtain the following statement.

Proposition 3.5.2
.

The functors of
eq.

3.5.3
are framings.

Remark 3.5.3
.

We recall from
section

3.2
that super bundle gerbes are obtained by applying the plus construction to the pre-2-stack
\mathscr{B}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}
. That pre-2-stack is trivially framed, i.e., the trivial functor

\ast\to\mathscr{B}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)

from the category
\ast
with a single object and a single morphism is a framing. Nonetheless, the construction
\mathscr{E}\mapsto\mathscr{E}^{\mathscr{F}}
generates from it a non-trivial presheaf of categories

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}:=\ast^{\mathscr{B}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}}\text{,}

together with a functor

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}\to\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{k}\text{.}

This is the “usual” framing of the 2-stack of bundle gerbes, i.e., the one whose morphisms are refinements of bundle gerbes, or “non-stable morphisms”.
The (trivially) commutative diagram

\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{B}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}}

expresses that the vertical arrows form a
morphism of framed pre-2-stacks
. Going from
\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{F}
to
\mathscr{E}^{\mathscr{F}}\to\mathscr{F}^{+}
is functorial; hence, the diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ss}\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}}

is commutative, too. Again, we may say that the vertical arrows, the passage from super line bundle gerbes to super 2-vector bundles, form a
morphism of framed 2-stacks
.

Remark 3.5.4
.

We recall from
section

3.3
that super algebra bundles (a.k.a. preliminary super 2-vector bundles) are examples of super 2-vector bundles, and that we have a pre-2-stack morphism

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}\text{.}

The bicategories
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
and
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
of preliminary super 2-vector bundles are themselves framed under the categories
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k})
and
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}
, respectively.
These framings are compatible with the ones given by refinements, in the sense of functors

\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k})\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{inv}\text{-}\mathrm{ref}}_{k}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}\to\mathrm{ss}\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}\text{.}

On the level of objects, these functors regard a super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
as a super 2-vector bundle like in (
3.3.1
). On the level of morphisms, they send a super algebra bundle homomorphism
\phi:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
to the refinement
\mathscr{R}_{\phi}:=(\operatorname{id}_{X},\phi,\phi)
. The diagrams

\textstyle{\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{inv}\text{-}\mathrm{ref}}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}}

\textstyle{\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ss}\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}}

obviously commute; here, the vertical arrows go from super algebra bundles to super 2-vector bundles, and the horizontal arrows are the framings. In other words, the passage from super algebra bundles to super 2-vector bundles is a morphism between framed pre-2-stacks.

3.6
Symmetric monoidal structures

The plus construction automatically extends (symmetric) monoidal structures from pre-2-stacks to 2-stacks. Unfortunately, this has not been discussed in
[
NS11
]
; we will describe this in full generality elsewhere.
The rationale is to go to common refinements of surjective submersions, and then to use the given symmetric monoidal structure of the pre-2-stack.

While the pre-2-stack
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
is
not
symmetric monoidal (recall that the heart of the problem is that the exterior tensor product of implementable bimodules need not be implementable again), we described in
section

2.2
the two sub-pre-2-stacks
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k})
and
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
that
are
symmetric monoidal.
Applying the plus construction equips the 2-stacks

\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}

with symmetric monoidal structures.
We remark that, if
\mathscr{V}
and
\mathscr{W}
are two super 2-vector bundles, their tensor product
\mathscr{V}\otimes\mathscr{W}
is always defined (in
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k})
), and it coincides with their tensor product in
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}
whenever
\mathscr{V}
and
\mathscr{W}
are semisimple. In other words, the problems only arise from the tensor product of 1-morphisms.

To describe the tensor product of super 2-vector bundles explicitly, let
\mathscr{V}_{1}=(\pi_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{1},\mu_{1})
and
\mathscr{V}_{2}=(\pi_{2},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{M}_{2},\mu_{2})
be super 2-vector bundles over
X
. Their tensor product
\mathscr{V}_{1}\otimes\mathscr{V}_{2}
has the surjective submersion
Y_{12}:=Y_{1}\times_{X}Y_{2}\to X
, the super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{A}_{2}
over
Y_{12}
(here, according to our conventions, the pullbacks are suppressed), the bimodule bundle
\mathcal{M}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}
over
Y_{12}^{[2]}
, and the intertwiner
\mu_{1}\otimes\mu_{2}
over
Y_{13}^{[3]}
. The trivial 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{I}
is the tensor unit.

The following statement is trivial, but worthwhile to state explicitly.

Proposition 3.6.1
.

Let
\mathscr{V},\mathscr{W}
be super 2-vector bundle over
X
, let
\mathscr{V}
be of Morita class
A
and
\mathscr{W}
be of Morita class
B
. Then
\mathscr{V}\otimes\mathscr{W}
is of Morita class
A\otimes B
.

The next statement gives, in particular, another justification for the terminology “2-line bundles”.

Proposition 3.6.2
.

Let
X
be a smooth manifold.

1.

Every semisimple super 2-vector bundle is fully dualizable in
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
.

2.

A semisimple super 2-vector bundle is invertible in
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)
if and only if it is a super 2-line bundle.

3.

The following are equivalent for a super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}
:

(a)

\mathscr{V}
is dualizable in
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))
.

(b)

\mathscr{V}
is fully dualizable in
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))
.

(c)

\mathscr{V}
is invertible in
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))
.

(d)

\mathscr{V}
is a super 2-line bundle.

One can construct from any super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}
a dual super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}^{*}
in a completely natural way: if
\mathcal{A}
is the super algebra bundle of
\mathscr{V}
, then the opposite algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{op}}
is the algebra bundle of
\mathscr{V}^{*}
.
For a detailed construction of
\mathscr{V}^{*}
we refer to
[
Mer20
, Rem. 2.1.14]
.
Similarly, it is unproblematic to construct evaluation and coevaluation 1-morphisms, whose bimodule bundles have the underlying vector bundle
\mathcal{A}
, considered as an
(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{op}})
-
\underline{k}
-bimodule bundle and a
\underline{k}
-
(\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{op}}\otimes\mathcal{A})
-bimodule bundle, respectively. Since
\mathcal{A}
is semisimple, these bimodule bundles have adjoints by
proposition

2.2.7
, and hence by
item

2
, the corresponding 1-morphisms in
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}(X)
have adjoints, too.
This shows (a). When
\mathcal{A}
is central simple, then by
propositions

2.2.7
and
1
we see that evaluation and coevaluation are invertible; this shows “if” of (b). The “only if” in (b) follows by restricting to connected components and looking at the Morita class: if
\mathscr{V}
is invertible, then by
propositions

3.6.1
and
3.1.2
its Morita class
A
must be invertible, too; hence
A
is central simple and
\mathscr{V}
is a line 2-bundle.

In (c), 1. – 3. are equivalent because
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))
is a 2-groupoid. The equivalence of 3. and 4. is seen as in (b).
∎

Remark 3.6.3
.

The inclusion
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{k}\to\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}
of super line bundle gerbes, a well as the inclusion
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}\to\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}
of semisimple super algebra bundles, are symmetric monoidal.

The symmetric monoidal pre-2-stacks
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k})
and
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}
have the direct sum as a second symmetric monoidal structure (
remark

2.2.8
), which induces analogously symmetric monoidal structures on
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k})
and
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}
.
The monoidal unit is the zero super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{O}
, which is the image of the zero super algebra bundle under the inclusion of algebra bundles.
Thus, it has the trivial cover
\operatorname{id}_{X}
, the zero algebra bundle, the zero bimodule bundle (note that this is invertible as a bimodule bundle between zero algebras), and the zero map.
Note that, unlike the tensor unit
\mathscr{I}
, the zero 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{O}
is not a bundle gerbe.

The following statement is again obvious.

Lemma 3.6.4
.

Let
\mathscr{V},\mathscr{W}
be super 2-vector bundle over
X
, let
\mathscr{V}
be of Morita class
A
and
\mathscr{W}
be of Morita class
B
. Then,
\mathscr{V}\oplus\mathscr{W}
is of Morita class
A\oplus B
.

A perhaps stunning application of the direct sum is that – within 2-vector bundles – one may now take the direct sum of two bundle gerbes. Recall that bundle gerbes are 2-line bundles and have Morita class
k
. The direct sum of two bundle gerbes is of Morita class
k\oplus k
and is hence not a 2-
line
bundle anymore.

3.7
Endomorphisms and automorphisms

If
\mathscr{V}
is a super 2-vector bundle, then we write
\mathscr{E}\!\mathrm{nd}(\mathscr{V}):=\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}(\mathscr{V},\mathscr{V})
for the category of endomorphisms, which is monoidal under the composition. We first want to compute the endomorphisms of the trivial 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{I}
.

We recall from
section

3.2
that we have fully faithful morphisms

\mathscr{B}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}\to\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{k}\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}

of presheaves of bicategories, under which the trivial 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{I}
is the image of the single object
\ast
in
\mathscr{B}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}
. In particular, we have an equivalence of monoidal categories

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)=\mathscr{E}\!\mathrm{nd}_{\mathscr{B}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}}(\ast)\cong\mathscr{E}\!\mathrm{nd}_{\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}}(\mathscr{I})\text{.}

We recall from
section

3.3
that we also have a fully faithful morphism

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}

of presheaves of bicategories, under which the trivial 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{I}
over a manifold
X
is the image of the trivial super algebra bundle
\underline{k}
over
X
.
In particular, we have again an equivalence of monoidal categories

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)\cong\mathscr{E}\!\mathrm{nd}_{\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{k}}(\underline{k})\cong\mathscr{E}\!\mathrm{nd}_{\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}}(\mathscr{I})\text{.}

It is straightforward to see from the various definitions that both equivalences coincide, and both give the same functor

H:\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)\to\mathscr{E}\!\mathrm{nd}_{\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}}(\mathscr{I})
: if
\mathcal{V}
is a super vector bundle over
X
, then the corresponding 1-morphism
H_{\mathcal{V}}:\mathscr{I}\to\mathscr{I}
has the identity surjective submersion on
Z:=X\times_{X}X\cong X
, it has the bimodule bundle
\mathcal{V}
, on which the super algebra bundle
\underline{k}
of
\mathscr{I}
acts fibrewise from both sides by scalar multiplication. Moreover, a morphism
\varphi:\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{W}
of super vector bundles induces in a straightforward way a 2-morphism
H_{\mathcal{V}}\to H_{\mathcal{W}}
. We summarize above considerations in the following lemma.

Lemma 3.7.1
.

The functor
H
establishes an equivalence of monoidal categories

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)\cong\mathscr{E}\!\mathrm{nd}(\mathscr{I})\text{.}

Now let
\mathscr{V}
be any super 2-vector bundle over
X
, which we take to be semisimple at first.
Suppose
\mathcal{E}
is a super vector bundle over
X
.
Using the functor
H
and the tensor product in
\mathrm{ss\text{-}s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}
, we define a 1-morphism
H_{\mathscr{V}}(\mathcal{E}):\mathscr{V}\to\mathscr{V}
by

\textstyle{\mathscr{V}\cong\mathscr{V}\otimes\mathscr{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{V}}\otimes H(\mathcal{E})}
\textstyle{\mathscr{V}\otimes\mathscr{I}\cong\mathscr{V}\text{.}}

Likewise, if
\varphi:\mathcal{E}_{1}\to\mathcal{E}_{2}
is a morphism of super vector bundles, then we define 2-morphism
H_{\mathscr{V}}(\varphi):H_{\mathscr{V}}(\mathcal{E}_{1})\Rightarrow H_{\mathscr{V}}(\mathcal{E}_{2})
in the obvious way using
\operatorname{id}_{\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{V}}}\otimes H(\varphi)
. This defines a monoidal functor

H_{\mathscr{V}}:\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)\to\mathscr{E}\!\mathrm{nd}(\mathscr{V})\text{,}

which coincides in the case of
\mathscr{V}=\mathscr{I}
with the functor
H
.

Explicitly, if
\mathscr{V}=(\pi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
is the super 2-vector bundle, then the 1-morphism
H_{\mathscr{V}}(\mathcal{E})
has the covering space
Z:=Y^{[2]}
, the identity surjective submersion
\zeta:=\operatorname{id}_{Z}
, and the bimodule bundle over
Z
is
\mathcal{M}\otimes_{k}\mathcal{E}
, where
\mathcal{E}
is understood to be pulled back along the projection
Z\to X
.
The 2-morphism
H_{\mathscr{V}}(\varphi):H_{\mathscr{V}}(\mathcal{E}_{1})\Rightarrow H_{\mathscr{V}}(\mathcal{E}_{2})
is given by the intertwiner
\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{M}}\otimes\varphi:\mathcal{M}\otimes_{k}\mathcal{E}_{1}\to\mathcal{M}\otimes_{k}\mathcal{E}_{2}
.

From this explicit description, it is clear that such a functor
H_{\mathscr{V}}
exists for
any
super vector bundle
\mathscr{V}
(not just semisimple ones), as the typical fibre
M\otimes_{k}E
of
\mathcal{M}\otimes_{k}\mathcal{E}
is implementing if
\mathcal{M}
is; see
[
KLW
, Ex. 3.1.5 (2)]
.

Lemma 3.7.2
.

The functor
H_{\mathscr{V}}
is faithful.

Suppose
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
are super vector bundles, and
\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}:\mathcal{E}_{1}\to\mathcal{E}_{2}
are super vector bundle homomorphisms. An equality
H_{\mathscr{V}}(\varphi)=H_{\mathscr{V}}(\varphi^{\prime})
between 2-morphisms implies an equality
\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{M}}\otimes\varphi_{1}=\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{M}}\otimes\varphi_{2}
between linear maps
\mathcal{M}\otimes_{k}\mathcal{E}_{1}\to\mathcal{M}\otimes_{k}\mathcal{E}_{2}
, and since these are finite-dimensional vector spaces, this implies
\varphi_{1}=\varphi_{2}
.
This shows that
H_{\mathscr{V}}
is faithful.
∎

Let us now specialize to
automorphism 2-groups
.
We recall that a (weak) 2-group is a monoidal groupoid in which every object is invertible with respect to the tensor product.
One example of a 2-group is
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))
, the groupoidification of the monoidal category of super line bundles over
X
. The 2-group
\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))
is additionally
symmetric
monoidal, i.e., a Picard groupoid.

If
\mathscr{C}
is a bicategory, and
c
is an object in
\mathscr{C}
, then its automorphism 2-group

\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(c):=\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathscr{E}\!\mathrm{nd}(c)^{\times})

is obtained from the monoidal category
\mathscr{E}\!\mathrm{nd}(c)
by discarding all non-invertible objects (i.e., all non-invertible 1-morphisms
c\to c
), and by discarding all non-invertible morphisms (i.e., all 2-morphisms in
\mathscr{C}
that are not invertible under vertical composition).
Note that
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(c)
is in general not
symmetric
monoidal.

By this construction, every super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}
over
X
has an automorphism 2-group
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(\mathscr{V})
. For a general super 2-vector bundle,
lemma

3.7.2
implies that the functor
H_{\mathscr{V}}
induces a faithful monoidal functor

\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))\to\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(\mathscr{V})\text{.}

By
lemma

3.7.1
, this functor is an equivalence in case of the trivial 2-vector bundle,

\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))\cong\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(\mathscr{I})\text{,}

i.e., the automorphisms of
\mathscr{I}
are precisely the super line bundles.

Furthermore, a result from the theory of bundle gerbes shows in fact that the automorphism 2-group of
every
line bundle gerbe
\mathscr{G}
is
\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X)^{\operatorname{grpd}}
, see
[
Wal07a
, Thm. 2.5.4]
. The proof given there generalizes in a straightforward way to the super case. Since the functor
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{k}\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}
is fully faithful, this shows that we have another equivalence of 2-groups,

\mathrm{Grpd}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))\cong\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(\mathscr{G})\text{,}

for every super line bundle gerbe
\mathscr{G}
.

3.8
Equivariant 2-vector bundles

As explained in
[
NS11
, Prop. 2.8]
, presheaves (of bicategories) on the category of smooth manifolds extend canonically to presheaves on the category of Lie groupoids (with smooth functors). This holds, in particular, for super 2-vector bundles, so that we automatically have a notion of super 2-vector bundles
over Lie groupoids
. In particular, this applies to action groupoids and hence leads automatically to the correct notion of an
equivariant
super 2-vector bundle.

Let
G
be a Lie group and let
\rho:G\times X\to X
be a smooth action of
G
on a smooth manifold
X
. The corresponding action groupoid
X/\!/G
is a Lie groupoid with objects
X
, morphisms
G\times X
, source map
\operatorname{pr}_{X}:G\times X\to X
, target map
\rho
, and composition
(g_{2},g_{1}x)\circ(g_{1},x):=(g_{2}g_{1},x)
.

Definition 3.8.1

(Equivariant 2-vector bundle)
.

A
G
-equivariant super 2-vector bundle
over
X
is a super 2-vector bundle over the Lie groupoid
X/\!/G
.

Spelling out the details on the basis of
[
NS11
, Def. 2.5]
,
a
G
-equivariant super 2-vector bundle over
X
is a triple
(\mathscr{V},\mathscr{P},\phi)
consisting of a super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}
over
X
, a 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{P}:\operatorname{pr}_{X}^{*}\mathscr{V}\to\rho^{*}\mathscr{V}
of super 2-vector bundles over
G\times X
, and a 2-isomorphism

\phi:(\operatorname{id}\times\rho)^{*}\mathscr{P}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{23}^{*}\rho^{*}\mathscr{P}\Rightarrow(m\times\operatorname{id})^{*}\mathscr{P}

of 2-vector bundles over
G^{2}\times X
, where
m:G^{2}\to G
denotes the product of
G
, such that
\phi
satisfies a coherence condition over
G^{3}\times X
. This becomes more instructive when restricted to single group elements: if
g\in G
, then pulling back
\mathscr{P}
along
X\to G\times X:x\mapsto(g,x)
yields a 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{P}_{g}:\mathscr{V}\to g^{*}\mathscr{V}
over
X
. Similarly, for two group elements
g_{1},g_{2}\in G
we obtain from
\phi
a 2-isomorphism
\phi_{g_{1},g_{2}}:g_{1}^{*}\mathscr{P}_{g_{2}}\circ\mathscr{P}_{g_{1}}\Rightarrow\mathscr{P}_{g_{1}g_{2}}
over
X
, and the coherence condition becomes

\phi_{g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}}\bullet(g_{1}^{*}\phi_{g_{2},g_{3}}\circ\operatorname{id})=\phi_{g_{1}g_{2},g_{3}}\bullet(\operatorname{id}\circ\phi_{g_{1},g_{2}})\text{.}

As explained in
[
NS11
, Def. 2.6]
, a whole bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}^{G}(X)
of
G
-equivariant super 2-vector bundles can be constructed in a canonical way.

Remark 3.8.2
.

The 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{P}
in the structure of a
G
-equivariant super 2-vector bundle may of course be (induced by) a refinement
\mathscr{R}
, see
section

3.5
. In this case, the 2-isomorphism
\phi
may be an equality, as refinements form a 1-category. Let us briefly spell out what such a
strict

G
-equivariant structure is; for this purpose we denote the involved structure by
\mathscr{V}=(\pi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
and
\mathscr{R}=(\tilde{\rho},\varphi,\phi)
. First,
\tilde{\rho}
induces a lift of the
G
-action along
\pi:Y\to X
, and hence,
G
-actions on all fibre products
Y^{[k]}
. Second,
\varphi
induces a
G
-equivariant structure on the super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
over
Y
. Third,
\phi
induces a compatible
G
-equivariant structure on the bimodule bundle
\mathcal{M}
over
Y^{[2]}
in such a way that the intertwiner
\mu
is
G
-equivariant.
Summarizing, a
G
-equivariant structure on a super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}
may in simple cases consist of lifts of the
G
-action to all of its structure.

Maybe the most useful statement about equivariant structure is that it descends to quotients whenever well-behaved quotients exist. In the present case, we have the following statement.

Theorem 3.8.3
.

Suppose a Lie group
G
acts freely and properly on a smooth manifold
X
.
Then, pullback along the projection
X\to X/G
induces an equivalence of bicategories between super 2-vector bundles on
X/G
and
G
-equivariant super 2-vector bundles on
X
,

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(G/X)\cong\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}^{G}(X)\text{.}

We derive this from the abstract theory developed in
[
NS11
]
and the fact that by our construction, super 2-vector bundles form a 2-stack. Suppose
\mathscr{F}
is a presheaf of bicategories on the category of smooth manifolds; we will denote its canonical extension to Lie groupoids by the same symbol. This is justified by the fact that, if
X
is a smooth manifold and
X_{dis}
denotes the discrete Lie groupoid with objects
X
and only identity morphisms, then
\mathscr{F}(X_{dis})\cong\mathscr{F}(X)
. Any smooth functor
F:\mathscr{X}\to\mathscr{Y}
between Lie groupoids induces a functor
F^{*}:\mathscr{F}(\mathscr{Y})\to\mathscr{F}(\mathscr{X})
between the corresponding bicategories. If
\mathscr{F}
is a 2-stack, and
F
is a weak equivalence, then
F^{*}
is an equivalence
\mathscr{F}(X)\cong\mathscr{F}(Y)

[
NS11
, Thm. 2.16]
. Now, in the present situation we consider the evident smooth functor
X/\!/G\to(X/G)_{dis}
, which is a weak equivalence as the projection
X\to X/G
is a surjective submersion. Combining and evaluating these facts for the 2-stack
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}
of super 2-vector bundles yields the claim.
∎

4
Classification of 2-vector bundles

In this section, we classify super 2-vector bundles of a fixed Morita class
A
, see
definition

3.1.5
. Our classification is based on an idea of Pennig
[
Pen
]
and uses the automorphism 2-group of a super algebra
A
. In the first two subsections, we neglect the monoidal structure and classify 2-vector bundles up to isomorphism as a set. The monoidal structure is then added in
section

4.3
.

Section

4.4
treats 2-line bundles, for which the classification simplifies.

4.1
Non-abelian cohomology for algebras

Let
A
be a Picard-surjective super algebra (see
section

2.1
). In
[
KLW
, §2.3]
we have shown that the automorphism 2-group of
A
as an object in the bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathrm{ect}_{k}
can be represented by a crossed module of Lie groups, denoted by
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
.
This crossed module will be central for the classification of super 2-vector bundles. We refer to
appendix

A
for a quick recollection of crossed modules.
The crossed module
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
consists of the Lie group
A^{\times}_{0}
of even invertible elements of
A
and of the Lie group
\mathrm{Aut}(A)
of even automorphisms of
A
, together with the Lie group homomorphism
i:A_{0}^{\times}\to\mathrm{Aut}(A)
that associates to an element
a\in A_{0}^{\times}
the conjugation
i(a)
by
a
, and the action of
\mathrm{Aut}(A)
on
A_{0}^{\times}
by evaluation.

The Čech cohomology of
X
with values in the crossed module
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
is (see
definition

A.2
),

\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)):=\mathrm{h}_{0}(\underline{\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(A)^{+}(X))\text{,}

(4.1.1)

i.e., we consider the Lie 2-groupoid
\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
with a single object that is associated to the crossed module
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
, the presheaf of bicategories
\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 21.75163pt}}}\end{picture}\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}\hskip-0.42677pt(A)
represented by
\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
, apply the plus-construction, evaluate on
X
, and then take the set of isomorphism classes of objects.
Spelling this out, see
appendix

A
, an element is represented with respect to an open cover
\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in A}
by a pair
(\varphi,a)
where
\varphi
is a collection of smooth maps
\varphi_{\alpha\beta}:U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\to\mathrm{Aut}(A)
and
a
is a collection of smooth maps
a_{\alpha\beta\gamma}:U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\cap U_{\gamma}\to A_{0}^{\times}
, such that the cocycle conditions

i(a_{\alpha\beta\gamma})\circ\varphi_{\beta\gamma}\circ\varphi_{\alpha\beta}=\varphi_{\alpha\gamma}\quad\text{ and }\quad a_{\alpha\gamma\delta}\cdot\varphi_{\gamma\delta}(a_{\alpha\beta\gamma})=a_{\alpha\beta\delta}\cdot a_{\beta\gamma\delta}

(4.1.2)

are satisfied. Two cocycles
(\varphi,a)
and
(\varphi^{\prime},a^{\prime})
are equivalent, if, after passing to a common refinement of the open covers, there exist smooth maps
\varepsilon_{\alpha}:U_{\alpha}\to\mathrm{Aut}(A)
and
e_{\alpha\beta}:U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\to A^{\times}
satisfying

i(e_{\alpha\beta})\circ\varepsilon_{\beta}\circ\varphi_{\alpha\beta}=\varphi^{\prime}_{\alpha\beta}\circ\varepsilon_{\alpha}\quad\text{ and }\quad a^{\prime}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\cdot\varphi^{\prime}_{\beta\gamma}(e_{\alpha\beta})\cdot e_{\beta\gamma}=e_{\alpha\gamma}\cdot\varepsilon_{\gamma}(a_{\alpha\beta\gamma})\text{.}

We note the following result about the Čech cohomology of Picard-surjective super algebras.

Proposition 4.1.1
.

Suppose
A
and
B
are Picard-surjective super algebras. Then, every invertible
A
-
B
-bimodule induces a bijection
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A))\cong\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(B))
. In particular, these sets coincide whenever
A
and
B
are Morita equivalent Picard surjective super algebras.

By
[
KLW
, Prop 2.3.3]
, any invertible
A
-
B
-bimodule
M
determines an invertible butterfly between the crossed modules
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
and
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(B)
, see
appendix

A
.
Weak equivalences induce bijections in cohomology, see
proposition

A.5
.
∎

4.2
The classification

Let
A
be a super algebra. We consider the presheaf of bicategories
A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}
introduced in
item

1
, consisting of super algebra bundles whose fibres are Morita equivalent to
A
, all implementing bimodule bundles and all even intertwiners. On the other hand, we consider the presheaf of bicategories
\underline{\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(A)
mentioned in
section

4.1
.
For a smooth manifold
X
, the bicategory
\underline{\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(A)(X)
has a single object, the 1-morphisms are smooth maps
X\to\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
, and the 2-morphisms are smooth maps
X\to A_{0}^{\times}\times\mathrm{Aut}(A)
. Here, a pair
(a,\varphi)
with
a:X\to A_{0}^{\times}
and
\varphi:X\to\mathrm{Aut}(A)
, is a 2-morphism from
x\mapsto\varphi(x)
to
x\mapsto i(a(x))\circ\varphi(x)
. The vertical composition of 2-morphisms is
(a_{2},\varphi_{2})\circ(a_{1},\varphi_{1}):=(a_{2}a_{1},\varphi_{1})
. The horizontal composition of 1-morphisms is given by the group structure on
\mathrm{Aut}(A)
, and the one of 2-morphisms is given by the semi-direct product
(a_{2},\varphi_{2})\cdot(a_{1},\varphi_{1}):=(a_{2}\varphi_{2}(a)),\varphi_{2}\circ\varphi_{1}
; see
appendix

A
for the general description.

In the following, we describe a morphism

F:\underline{\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(A)\to A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}

(4.2.1)

of presheaves of bicategories.
Over a smooth manifold
X
,
it sends the unique object
of
\underline{\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(A)(X)
to the trivial algebra bundle
F_{X}(\ast):=\underline{A}
.
It sends a 1-morphism, i.e., a smooth map
\varphi:X\to\mathrm{Aut}(A)
to the (implementing) bimodule bundle
F_{X}(\varphi):=\mathcal{M}_{\varphi}:=\underline{A}_{\varphi}
(see
example

2.2.4
).
Finally, it sends a 2-morphism, i.e., a pair
(a,\varphi)
of a smooth map
a:X\to A^{\times}
and a smooth map
\varphi:X\to\mathrm{Aut}(A)
,
to the intertwiner
F_{X}(a,\varphi):=\phi_{a,\varphi}:\mathcal{M}_{\varphi}\to\mathcal{M}_{i(a)\circ\varphi}
given in each fibre by the canonical isomorphism

A_{\varphi_{x}}\cong A\otimes_{A}A_{\varphi_{x}}\cong A_{i(a(x))}\otimes_{A}A_{\varphi_{x}}\cong A_{i(a(x))\circ\varphi_{x}}\text{;}

explicitly,
\phi_{a,\varphi}(x,b):=(x,ba^{-1})
.

Lemma 4.2.1
.

Above definitions yield a 2-functor
F_{X}:\underline{\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(A)(X)\to A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}(X)
.

F_{X}
preserves the composition of 1-morphisms under the compositor

\mathcal{M}_{\varphi_{2}}\circ\mathcal{M}_{\varphi_{1}}\cong\mathcal{M}_{\varphi_{2}\circ\varphi_{1}},

given fibrewise by the map
A_{\varphi_{2}}\otimes_{A}A_{\varphi_{1}}\cong A_{\varphi_{2}\circ\varphi_{1}}:a\otimes b\mapsto a\varphi_{2}(b)
. Moreover,

F_{X}
preserves the vertical composition of 2-morphisms because of the equality
\phi_{a_{2},i(a_{1})\circ\varphi}\circ\phi_{a_{1},\varphi}=\phi_{a_{2}a_{1},\varphi}
of intertwiners, which follows immediately from the definition of
\phi_{a,\varphi}
.

It is less obvious that
F_{X}
preserves the horizontal composition of 2-morphisms. Consider two 2-morphisms
(a_{1},\varphi_{1}):\varphi_{1}\Rightarrow i(a_{1})\circ\varphi_{1}
and
(a_{2},\varphi_{2}):\varphi_{2}\Rightarrow i(a_{2})\circ\varphi_{2}
, whose horizontal composition is, cf.
eq.

A.1
:

(a_{2}\varphi_{2}(a_{1}),\varphi_{2}\circ\varphi_{1}):\varphi_{2}\circ\varphi_{1}\Rightarrow i(a_{2}\varphi_{2}(a_{1}))\circ\varphi_{2}\circ\varphi_{1}\text{.}

On the other side, we consider the images under
F_{X}
, i.e., the intertwiners
\phi_{a_{1},\varphi_{1}}:\mathcal{M}_{\varphi_{1}}\to\mathcal{M}_{i(a_{1})\circ\varphi_{1}}
and
\phi_{a_{2},\varphi_{2}}:\mathcal{M}_{\varphi_{2}}\to\mathcal{M}_{i(a_{2})\circ\varphi_{2}}
and their tensor product

\phi_{a_{2},\varphi_{2}}\otimes\phi_{a_{1},\varphi_{1}}:\mathcal{M}_{\varphi_{2}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{M}_{\varphi_{2}}\to\mathcal{M}_{i(a_{2})\circ\varphi_{2}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{M}_{i(a_{1})\circ\varphi_{1}}\text{.}

The condition that this corresponds, under the compositors, to
\phi_{a_{2}\varphi_{2}(a_{1}),\varphi_{2}\circ\varphi_{1}}
is the commutativity of the diagram

\textstyle{A_{\varphi_{2}}\otimes_{A}A_{\varphi_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{i(a_{2})\circ\varphi_{2}}\otimes_{A}A_{i(a_{1})\circ\varphi_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{\varphi_{2}\circ\varphi_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{i(a_{2}\varphi_{2}(a_{1}))\circ\varphi_{2}\circ\varphi_{1}}}

\textstyle{b\otimes c\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{ba_{2}^{-1}\otimes ca_{1}^{-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{b\varphi_{2}(c)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{b\varphi_{2}(c)\varphi_{2}(a_{1})^{-1}a_{2}^{-1}\text{.}}

This is indeed commutative; this finishes the proof that
F_{X}
is a 2-functor.
∎

It is obvious that
F_{X}
is compatible with the pullback along smooth maps between contractible manifolds; hence
X\mapsto F_{X}
is indeed a morphism
F
of presheaves of bicategories.

Let
\mathrm{Grpd}(A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}})
denote the 2-groupoidification of the presheaf of super algebra bundles of Morita class
A
, obtained by discarding all non-invertible 1-morphisms and all non-invertible intertwiners.
By
[
KLW
, Lemmas 2.1.3 and 4.2.8]
, the morphism
F
factors through the inclusion
\mathrm{Grpd}(A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}})\subseteq A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}
. We denote by
c\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}\subseteq\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}
the full subcategory of all smooth manifolds with all connected components contractible, and infer the following result.

Lemma 4.2.2
.

Suppose
A
is a Picard-surjective super algebra.
Then, the morphism
F
of
eq.

4.2.1
induces an isomorphism

\underline{\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(A)|_{c\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}\cong\mathrm{Grpd}(A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}})|_{c\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}

between the restrictions of both presheaves to the subcategory
c\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}\subseteq\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}
.

It suffices to show that the 2-functor
F_{X}
is an equivalence of bicategories whenever
X
is contractible.
That
F_{X}
is essentially surjective comes from the fact that algebra bundles over contractible manifolds are trivializable. This can be proved analogously to the corresponding fact for vector bundles, see
[
Hat17
, Prop. 1.7 and Cor. 1.8]
. Next we prove that the Hom-functor

F_{X}(\ast,\ast):\underline{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(A)(X)\to\underline{A}\text{-}\underline{A}\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{im}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)^{\times}

is an equivalence of categories. Here,
(..)^{\times}
denotes the subcategory of invertible bimodule bundles and invertible intertwiners, which appears here because of the 2-groupoidification.
First of all, by the same argument as before, every
\underline{A}
-
\underline{A}
-bimodule bundle is trivializable as a vector bundle over the contractible manifold
X
, and hence, by
definition

2.2.3
, of the form
\prescript{}{\phi}{}\underline{M}_{\psi}
, where
M
is an (invertible)
A
-
A
-bimodule and
\phi,\psi:X\to\mathrm{Aut}(A)
are smooth maps.
As
A
is Picard-surjective, we have
M\cong A_{\varphi_{0}}
for some
\varphi_{0}\in\mathrm{Aut}(A)
.
We obtain an isomorphism of module bundles

\prescript{}{\phi}{}\underline{M}_{\psi}\cong\prescript{}{\phi}{}\underline{A}_{\varphi_{0}\circ\psi}\cong\underline{A}_{\phi^{-1}\circ\varphi_{0}\circ\psi}\text{,}

where the last isomorphism is obtained by applying the isomorphism of bimodules
\prescript{}{\varphi}{}A\mapsto A_{\varphi^{-1}}
,
a\mapsto\varphi^{-1}(a)
fibrewise.
This shows that
F_{X}(\ast,\ast)
is essentially surjective. Finally, on the level of 2-morphisms, that
F_{X}
is fully faithful follows from
[
KLW
, Lemma 2.1.3 (d)]
.
∎

If two presheaves of bicategories become isomorphic when restricted to the subcategory
c\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}
of manifolds with contractible connected components, then the plus construction will associate to them
isomorphic
presheaves. The reason for this is the existence of good open covers on manifolds, in combination with
lemma

2.3.8
. More precisely, let us denote by
\mathscr{F}^{c+}
a variant of the plus construction in which the domains
Y
,
Z
,
W
of all surjective submersions that appear in the description given in
section

2.3
are objects of
c\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}
. On one side, we obtain an evident inclusion
\mathscr{F}^{c+}\to\mathscr{F}^{+}
of presheaves, and we claim that this is an isomorphism. For instance, essential surjectivity follows from
lemma

2.3.8
by choosing a good open cover of
X
with local sections into the surjective submersion
\pi:Y\to X
of a given super 2-vector bundle. Analogous considerations show essential surjectivity on the level of 1-morphisms and surjectivity on the level of 2-morphisms. On the other side, the presheaf
\mathscr{F}^{c+}
evaluates the given presheaf
\mathscr{F}
only on objects of
c\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}
. This proves above claim, and thus,
lemma

4.2.2
implies the following result.

Proposition 4.2.3
.

Let
A
be a Picard-surjective super algebra. Then, the morphism
F
of
eq.

4.2.1
induces an isomorphism of 2-stacks

\underline{\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(A)^{+}\cong\mathrm{Grpd}(A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}})^{+}\text{.}

Now we are in position to present our classification theorem.

Theorem 4.2.4
.

For any Picard-surjective super algebra
A
, there is a canonical bijection

\mathrm{h}_{0}(A\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X))\cong\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)).

In other words, super 2-vector bundles over
X
of Morita class
A
are classified by the Čech cohomology of
X
with values in the crossed module
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
.

We note that

\displaystyle\mathrm{h}_{0}(A\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{h}_{0}((A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}})^{+}(X))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{h}_{0}(\mathrm{Grpd}((A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}})^{+}(X)))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{h}_{0}(\mathrm{Grpd}(A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}})^{+}(X))\text{,}

where the first equality holds by definition of super 2-vector bundles, and the second holds because 2-groupoidification preserves the set of isomorphism classes of objects, and the third holds because 2-groupoidification commutes with the plus construction (this follows from
lemma

2.3.4
).
The claim follows then from
proposition

4.2.3
and
eq.

4.1.1
.
∎

Remark 4.2.5
.

The condition of being Picard-surjective can be achieved at any time by passing to a Morita equivalent super algebra: this is possible because every super algebra is Morita equivalent to a Picard-surjective one (
[
KLW
, Prop. A.2]
) and
\mathrm{h}_{0}(A\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X))
is Morita invariant by
lemma

3.1.2
.

A result of Baez and Stevenson
[
BS09
]
shows that the geometric realization
|\Gamma|
of (the Lie 2-group associated to) a crossed module
\Gamma
is a topological group, whose classifying space
\mathrm{B}|\Gamma|
represents the cohomology with values in
\Gamma
,

\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\Gamma)\cong[X,\mathrm{B}|\Gamma|].

Combining this with
theorem

4.2.4
, we obtain the following.

Corollary 4.2.6
.

For any Picard-surjective super algebra
A
,
\mathrm{B}|\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)|
is a classifying space for super 2-vector bundles of Morita class
A
.

For the convenience of the reader, we shall spell out explicitly procedures that realize the bijection of
theorem

4.2.4
, obtained by passing through all intermediate steps described above.
Given a cocycle
(\varphi,a)
in
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A))
, we construct the following super 2-vector bundle over
X
.
Its surjective submersion
Y
is the disjoint union of the open sets
U_{\alpha}
of the cover on which
(\varphi,a)
is defined. The algebra bundle is the trivial algebra bundle
\underline{A}
over
Y
.
Over
Y^{[2]}
, which is the disjoint union of double intersections
U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}
, we have the map
\varphi:Y^{[2]}\to\mathrm{Aut}(A)
, to which we associate the bimodule bundle
\underline{A}_{\varphi}
, the fibre of which is
A_{\varphi_{x}}
.
Over
Y^{[3]}
, we have the three automorphisms
\varphi_{12}
,
\varphi_{23}
and
\varphi_{13}
defined by
\varphi_{ij}:=\operatorname{pr}_{ij}^{*}\varphi
, and we have
i(a)\circ\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12}=\varphi_{13}
due to the cocycle condition, where
a:Y^{[3]}\to A^{\times}
.
At each point,
a
defines an invertible even intertwiner of
A
-
A
-bimodules

\underline{A}_{\varphi_{23}}\otimes_{\underline{A}}\underline{A}_{\varphi_{12}}\cong\underline{A}_{\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12}}\cong\underline{A}\otimes_{\underline{A}}\underline{A}_{\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12}}\cong\underline{A}_{i(a)}\otimes_{\underline{A}}\underline{A}_{\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12}}\cong\underline{A}_{i(a)\circ\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12}}\cong\underline{A}_{\varphi_{13}}\text{.}

These form the required associative intertwiner
\mu:\underline{A}_{\varphi_{23}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}\underline{A}_{\varphi_{12}}\to\underline{A}_{\varphi_{13}}
of bimodule bundles over
Y^{[3]}
.

Conversely, given a super 2-vector bundle
\mathscr{V}=(\pi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
in
A\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)
, we
may first pass via
proposition

2.3.9
to an isomorphic one where
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{M}
are trivial in the sense of
remarks

2.2.2
and
2.2.4
.
On each connected component
X_{i}
, the trivial super algebra bundle has a typical fibre
A_{i}
which is Morita equivalent to
A
.
Choosing invertible
A
-
A_{i}
-bimodules
M_{i}
, one can construct a 1-isomorphism that takes us to a super 2-vector bundle whose super algebra bundle is the trivial bundle
\underline{A}
over
Y
and whose bimodule bundle is still trivial.
Because of Picard-surjectivity of
A
, we may then identify the bimodule bundle with
\underline{A}_{\varphi}
, where
\varphi
is a map
\varphi:Y^{[2]}\to\mathrm{Aut}(A)
(see the argument in the proof of
lemma

4.2.2
). We write again
\varphi_{ij}:=\operatorname{pr}_{ij}^{*}\varphi
. Over
Y^{[3]}
, we obtain an invertible even intertwiner

\mu:\underline{A}_{\varphi_{23}}\otimes_{\underline{A}}\underline{A}_{\varphi_{12}}\to\underline{A}_{\varphi_{13}}

of
\underline{A}
-
\underline{A}
-bimodule bundles.
Under the isomorphism
\underline{A}_{\varphi_{23}}\otimes_{\underline{A}}\underline{A}_{\varphi_{12}}\cong\underline{A}_{\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12}}
, this becomes an even intertwiner
\underline{A}_{\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12}}\cong\underline{A}_{\varphi_{13}}
which by
[
KLW
, Lemma 2.1.3 (d)]
corresponds to a unique smooth map
a:Y^{[3]}\to A^{\times}
such that
i(a)\circ\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12}=\varphi_{13}
.
Finally, using
lemma

2.3.8
one can now achieve that
Y
is the disjoint union of open sets; then,
(\varphi,a)
is a cocycle representing the class of
\mathscr{V}
in
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A))
.

Remark 4.2.7
.

Ungraded algebras are rarely Picard surjective when regarded as super algebras concentrated in even degrees. For that reason, one cannot simply apply the results of this section to ungraded algebras. However, one can proceed in close analogy. An ungraded algebra is called
ungraded Picard-surjective
in the sense that every
ungraded
invertible
A
-
A
-bimodule is induced from an automorphism of
A
. The presheaf morphism
F
becomes a morphism

F:\underline{\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(A)\to A\text{-}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}

to
ungraded
algebra bundles. The ungraded analogue of

Lemma

4.2.2
holds, and hence
proposition

4.2.3
has as an ungraded analogue an isomorphism

\underline{\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(A)^{+}\cong\mathrm{Grpd}(A\text{-}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}})^{+}\text{.}

We obtain a classification result analogous to
theorem

4.2.4
,

\mathrm{h}_{0}(A\text{-}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X))\cong\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A))\text{.}

Likewise,
\mathrm{B}|\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)|
is a classifying space for ungraded 2-vector bundles.

To close this section, we discuss the canonical inclusion
\mathscr{B}Z(A)_{0}^{\times}\to\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
, its induced map in cohomology, and its geometric interpretation by super 2-vector bundles.
Here,
\mathscr{B}Z(A)_{0}^{\times}
denotes the crossed module
Z(A)_{0}^{\times}\to\ast
, with the trivial action.
The inclusion
\mathscr{B}Z(A)_{0}^{\times}\to\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
is the strict homomorphism of crossed modules given by the inclusion
Z(A)_{0}^{\times}\subseteq A_{0}^{\times}
. Note that the cohomology with values in the crossed module
\mathscr{B}Z(A)_{0}^{\times}
is the ordinary degree two Čech cohomology with values in the sheaf of smooth
Z(A)_{0}^{\times}
-valued functions (see
remark

A.3
).
Thus, the map induced by
\mathscr{B}Z(A)_{0}^{\times}\to\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
is a map

\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 20.54327pt}}}\end{picture}Z(A)\hskip-0.42677pt^{\times}_{0})\to\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A))\text{,}

and it sends a Čech cocycle
a_{\alpha\beta\gamma}:U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\cap U_{\gamma}\to Z(A)_{0}^{\times}
to the
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
-cocycle
(1,a)
.

For any abelian Lie group
K
, the cohomology group
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,K)
classifies
K
-principal bundle gerbes over
X
. These are principal bundle versions of the bundle gerbes discussed in
section

3.2
, and defined as

\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{K}:=\mathscr{B}(K\text{-}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl})^{+}\text{,}

where
K\text{-}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}
is the monoidal stack of principal
K
-bundles. In our case,
K=Z(A)_{0}^{\times}
comes with a monoidal functor

Z(A)_{0}^{\times}\text{-}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)\to A\text{-}A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{im}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)\text{,}

obtained by associating to a principal
Z(A)_{0}^{\times}
-bundle
\mathcal{Z}
the vector bundle
\mathcal{Z}\times_{Z(A)_{0}^{\times}}A
, which becomes a bundle of
A
-
A
-bimodules in the obvious way. In turn, we obtain
a morphism of presheaves
\mathscr{B}(Z(A)_{0}^{\times}\text{-}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl})\to A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}
, which under the plus construction yields a morphism

\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{Z(A)_{0}^{\times}}\to A\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}\text{,}

(4.2.2)

for any super algebra
A
. In particular, if
A
is central (i.e.,
Z(A)_{0}^{\times}=k^{\times}
) this 2-functor coincides with the 2-functor
eq.

3.2.3
.
The following result follows directly from either the cocycle description or the abstract stackification procedure.

Proposition 4.2.8
.

Let
A
be a Picard-surjective super algebra.
The map in cohomology induced by the inclusion
\mathscr{B}Z(A)_{0}^{\times}\to\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)
corresponds to the 2-functor (
4.2.2
) that sends principal
Z(A)_{0}^{\times}
-bundle gerbes to super 2-vector bundles. In other words, the diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{h_{0}}(\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{Z(A)_{0}^{\times}}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathrm{h_{0}}(A\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{k}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 20.54327pt}}}\end{picture}Z(A)\hskip-0.42677pt^{\times}_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A))}

is commutative.

4.3
Monoidal structure

Let
A
and
B
be super algebras. There is a strict homomorphism

m:\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)\times\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(B)\to\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A\otimes B)

(4.3.1)

of crossed modules, given by the maps

\displaystyle A_{0}^{\times}\times B_{0}^{\times}

\displaystyle\to(A\otimes B)_{0}^{\times};

\displaystyle(a,b)

\displaystyle\mapsto a\otimes b

\displaystyle\mathrm{Aut}(A)\times\mathrm{Aut}(B)

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Aut}(A\otimes B);

\displaystyle(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})

\displaystyle\mapsto\varphi_{1}\otimes\varphi_{2}

It induces a map in cohomology,

\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A))\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(B))\to\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A\otimes B))\text{.}

(4.3.2)

We want to show that this map corresponds to the tensor product of super 2-vector bundles.
We claim that the presheaf morphism

F:\underline{\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(A)\to A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}

of (
4.2.1
) is compatible with the tensor product of super algebras, in the sense that the diagram

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 60.94629pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.57121pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underline{\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(A)\times\underline{\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.54591pt\raise-16.2475pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.975pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{F\times F}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.50499pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.81364pt\raise 5.39168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{B}M}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 121.0228pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 121.0228pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underline{\mathscr{B}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}}(A\otimes B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 144.31618pt\raise-16.2475pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{F}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 144.31618pt\raise-23.50499pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-60.94629pt\raise-32.49501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}\times B\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.82132pt\raise-38.07835pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\otimes}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 100.94635pt\raise-32.49501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 100.94635pt\raise-32.49501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(A\otimes B)\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

(4.3.3)

of presheaves of bicategories is (strictly!) commutative.
On the level of objects, this is clear.
On the level of 1-morphisms, the diagram commutes since
\underline{A}_{\varphi_{1}}\otimes_{k}\underline{B}_{\varphi_{2}}=(\underline{A}\otimes_{k}\underline{B})_{\varphi_{1}\otimes\varphi_{2}}
(the identity on
\underline{A}\otimes\underline{B}
is an intertwiner).
On the level of 2-morphisms, the diagram commutes because the intertwiner
\phi_{a,\varphi}
in the definition of
F
on 2-morphisms satisfies (by inspection) the identity

\phi_{a,\varphi_{A}}\otimes\phi_{b,\varphi_{B}}=\phi_{a\otimes b,\varphi_{A}\otimes\varphi_{B}}\text{;}

this is precisely the coincidence between clockwise and counter-clockwise directions.

Proposition 4.3.1
.

For any pair of super algebras
A
and
B
, the diagram

\textstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A))\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(B))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F\times F}
\textstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A\otimes B))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{\mathrm{h}_{0}(A\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X))\times\mathrm{h}_{0}(B\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{h}_{0}((A\otimes B)\text{-}\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X))}

is commutative.

In view of
definition

A.2
, the commutativity of the diagram then follows from applying the (functorial) plus-construction to the diagram (
4.3.3
) and passing to isomorphism classes.
∎

4.4
Classification of super 2-line bundles

A central simple super algebra has by definition
Z(A)^{\times}_{0}=k^{\times}
, and
\mathrm{Pic}(A)=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, with representatives given by
A
and
\Pi A
, see
[
KLW
, Remark 2.2.5 (1)]
.
We consider the trivial crossed module
k^{\times}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, which is given by the zero map and the trivial action of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
on
k^{\times}
.
It can be written as a direct product crossed module
\mathscr{B}k^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{dis}
.

Lemma 4.4.1
.

If
A
is a Picard-surjective central simple super algebra over
k
, then there is a bijection

\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A))\cong\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\underline{k}^{\times})\text{.}

(4.4.1)

By
[
KLW
, Prop. 2.3.4]
there is a canonical weak equivalence
\mathscr{A}\mathrm{ut}(A)\cong\mathscr{B}k^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{dis}
, whose definition we recall briefly.
It is established by a butterfly (see
appendix

A
)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.98613pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.13542pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{k^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.98613pt\raise-31.78221pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 33.13542pt\raise-31.89778pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 79.68753pt\raise-55.56444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
